## 2026-10-01 (night, fourth) — EXECUTED: audit dispositions wired in order — operator persona + default model now drive the Ops Agent chat, real budget governance in the queue processor, fictional tabs relabeled (suite 549/549)

- **Trigger:** Robert approved the audit's recommended order (CC-2026-10-01-008): "I like and agree with that order. Please execute."
- **Wired:** (1) ORCHESTRATOR PERSONA — buildSystemPrompt now takes the workspace row's stored orchestrator system prompt as the LEAD of the base identity (built-in default when empty) plus the operator-assigned name; the fabricated "AgentLab DAG Orchestration Engine v2.4" string is REMOVED from the base prompt (the same fiction the conversational tail bans). (2) DEFAULT MODEL — the stored defaultModel LEADS the chat chain via a new withGoogleModelChainLeading helper (preferred-first, deduped, full-chain fallback); invalid/retired/dormant ids are skipped at the call site with a logged note instead of silently 404ing; the chat no longer hardcodes the chain head as modelUsed's default. (3) BUDGET GOVERNANCE — evaluateSpendGovernance reads workspaces.hard_monthly_budget + auto_pause_threshold_enabled, computes month-to-date token spend from real audit_logs rows (declared $0.25/M-token estimate constant), and the queue processor now AUTO-PAUSES runs of workspaces that are enabled and over budget, failing the run with the real reason and writing a BUDGET_AUTOPAUSE audit row (not-llm-dispatch); operator workspaces exempt. (4) SETTINGS HONESTY — Profile/Billing/Notifications tabs carry amber demo-only banners naming what is not real; Security badges gained verification tooltips and the dead Manage Auth button became a working Sign Out; the stdio MCP preset strip states those servers are not runtime-executable.
- **Verification:** 15 new tests (persona lead/fallback/name-clause/v2.4-exclusion 4; preferred-model chain first/fallback-dedup/plain-order/invalid-id 4; spend governance over/within/disabled/zero-budget/exempt/db-safe/estimate-constant 7); full suite 549/549 across 59 files; tsc at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline; change-control green.
- **Honest notes:** token spend is an ESTIMATE (per-model pricing is not recorded per row — the constant is declared and exported); PII/SAIF/retention toggles deliberately remain hardcoded (governance decision, not wiring); the twelve remaining localStorage LLM decorations stay cosmetic by design.
- **Recorded as:** CC-2026-10-01-009 (detail at bottom); audit doc statuses updated to DONE.

## 2026-10-01 (night, third) — SETTINGS WIRING AUDIT: every control traced; verdict — Secrets + Integrations now genuinely wired; LLM tab 3 stored-but-unconsumed + 12 fictional; Profile/Billing/Notifications/Security tabs fictional

(2) LLM tab: orchestrator name/system prompt/default model save to the workspaces row but have NO consumer — the chat uses buildSystemPrompt(departments, telemetry) (orchestrator.ts:575), ignoring the stored prompt; the other 12 controls (persona, tone, knowledge brain, temperature, top-p, token-budget cap, auto-pause, etc.) are localStorage-only decorations. (3) The workspaces table's REAL governance columns — hard_monthly_budget, auto_pause_threshold_enabled, pii_redaction_enabled, saif_enforcement_enabled, audit_retention_days — have ZERO consumers anywhere: the budget UI is decoration while genuine SAIF/PII protection exists as hardcoded pipeline behavior. (4) Profile, Billing, Notifications tabs save to localStorage only; Security tab has hardcoded "Active & Enforced" badges and a dead "Manage Auth" button.
- **Highest-leverage fix candidate:** wire the Orchestrator System Prompt into buildSystemPrompt (small, makes the Ops Agent persona operator-controllable). Recommendations table in the audit doc awaits Robert's dispositions; no code changed in this entry.
- **Recorded as:** CC-2026-10-01-008 (detail at bottom).

## 2026-10-01 (night, second) — SHIPPED: MCP wired end to end — Settings connect UI, vault round-trip, and Ops Agent tool exposure behind human-gated dispatch (suite 534/534)

- **Trigger:** Robert: "Let's go with your suggested Build MCP client + OAuth now" — the full slice after CC-2026-10-01-006's engine.
- **Shipped:** (1) `mcp-connect-state.ts` — HMAC-signed, expiring OAuth state carrying the PKCE verifier (never stored server-side, never guessable by the browser; timing-safe verify; 10-minute TTL). (2) `mcp-tokens.ts` — the request-path token resolver: integration-row catalog key first, then the mapped env key `MCP_<NAME>_TOKEN`, with an explicit connect-first error — never a silent fallback. (3) `mcp-connect.ts` — startConnect (live discovery + PKCE + signed state + authorize URL), completeConnect (verify state once → code exchange → token bundle into the vault pattern: real value via applySecretToEnv, masked-preview row in workspace_secrets, integration flips active), disconnect (best-effort revoke + token cleared + row inactive), refresh (uses the stored bundle's refresh_token under the same env key). Non-operator workspaces are refused at completion per the 2026-09-24 multi-tenant guard — no cross-tenant process.env writes. (4) `mcp-tool-bridge.ts` + the `mcp_tool_call` connector in the registry — the ONLY path from human approval to an MCP server: endpoint + token resolved per workspace (cross-workspace refused, stdio rejected), session opened, tool existence verified (failure names the server's actual tools), tool called, isError surfaced as dispatch failure. The drafter now offers it automatically (registry-generated list); the workspace id is injected AFTER payload validation so no draft can smuggle it. (5) Settings UI: Connect/Disconnect buttons per MCP row (real connected/not-connected badge), List Tools live probe, OAuth client id/secret fields on the mount form with the redirect-URI hint, and the provider callback (?code=&state=) auto-completes on return. (6) `testIntegration` for MCP now runs a REAL session probe — the old fictional "Verified integration profile" fallback can no longer fire for MCP.
- **Verification:** 23 new tests (state tamper/expiry/round-trip 5; connector registration + strict payload validation 4; bridge end-to-end/tool-not-found/stdio/cross-workspace/connect-first/401/isError/shape 8; token resolver precedence 6) — all hermetic; full suite 534/534 across 57 files; tsc at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline; change-control green.
- **Honest remaining gaps:** Upwork end-to-end needs Robert's developer-app registration (client id/secret + redirect URI `<origin>/settings`); non-operator per-workspace token storage is recorded as a follow-up (connect is refused honestly today); stdio servers remain unreachable by design; deploy is the manual Cloud Build path.
- **Recorded as:** CC-2026-10-01-007 (detail at bottom).

## 2026-10-01 (night) — SHIPPED: MCP OAuth module + MCP runtime client built, hermetically tested (15 new tests, suite 511/511), and proven against Upwork's LIVE metadata

- **Trigger:** Robert said go on "Build MCP client + OAuth now" (CC-2026-10-01-005) and reported the catalog split (6 stdio + 2 SSE — matching the DB probe exactly).
- **Shipped (server/execution/):** `mcp-oauth.ts` — metadata-driven OAuth 2.1: RFC 9728 protected-resource discovery (path-then-origin candidates), RFC 8414 authorization-server discovery (with origin fallback), PKCE S256 pair, authorize-URL builder (state + resource + scopes), code exchange (client_secret_post), refresh grant, revocation, expiry-margin check. `mcp-client.ts` — MCP streamable-HTTP JSON-RPC client: initialize + initialized notification, Mcp-Session-Id handling, Bearer + MCP-Protocol-Version headers, tools/list, tools/call with text flattening, SSE-framed response parsing, honest HTTP/JSON-RPC error surfacing. Tests: `mcp-oauth.test.ts` (9) + `mcp-client.test.ts` (6) — hermetic, local stub HTTP servers, zero network. Probe: `scripts/probe-mcp-discovery.ts` — ran the OS's OWN discovery module against Upwork's real server successfully.
- **Verification:** 15/15 new tests; full suite 511/511 (53 files); tsc at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline (zero new errors); live discovery probe SUCCESS on `https://mcp.upwork.com/mcp`.
- **Honest scope boundary:** this ships the engine, not the wiring. Settings UI connect flow, token vault round-trip in the request path, and Ops Agent tool exposure are the next slice; stdio catalog servers are NOT reachable by this client (documented in-file). Upwork needs Robert's developer-app registration before a real token exchange can run.
- **Recorded as:** CC-2026-10-01-006 (detail at bottom); queue row CC-2026-10-01-005 advanced.

## 2026-10-01 (evening) — DECISION: build the OS MCP runtime client + metadata-driven OAuth connect flow; catalog-vs-runtime distinction confirmed for the whole integrations list

- **Trigger:** Robert, on the CC-2026-10-01-004 finding: "If [the list is not active], then yes we need to build the MCP client and design the OAuth flow for MCP." He also added the Upwork MCP as a custom connector in Buffy's own client settings (not yet visible in the running session — connector sets load at session start).
- **Catalog clarification (answers Robert's question precisely):** the `workspace_integrations` list is NOT a connection layer — it is a design-time catalog. Capabilities that genuinely work (HubSpot sync, Gemini chain, ElevenLabs, AgentMail) run through vault secrets and dedicated code paths, not through the table; the table drives nothing. All eight MCP rows have zero runtime, and several hold placeholder endpoints. No existing capability is lost — but no MCP row represents anything runnable.
- **Upwork OAuth metadata captured (public discovery, verified live):** resource `https://mcp.upwork.com/mcp` (Bearer header); authorization server = itself; authorize `https://www.upwork.com/ab/account-security/oauth2/authorize`; token `https://www.upwork.com/api/v3/oauth2/token`; revoke `.../token/revoke`; grants `authorization_code` + `refresh_token` (+ client_credentials); PKCE S256; token auth `client_secret_post/basic/none/private_key_jwt`; `client_id_metadata_document_supported: true`.
- **Design direction (queued, not started):** GENERIC, metadata-driven — discover endpoints from `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` + `oauth-authorization-server`, authorize with PKCE from Settings, exchange code, store access+refresh tokens in the existing `workspaceSecrets` vault (masked preview in UI), refresh on expiry, revoke on disconnect; MCP runtime client speaks streamable-HTTP + SSE with Bearer header and exposes server tools to the Ops Agent. Upwork = first connector; any OAuth-gated MCP server connects the same way. Queued as CC-2026-10-01-005.
- **Recorded as:** CC-2026-10-01-005 (detail at bottom).

## 2026-10-01 (investigation) — ops_agent_messages is empty in production: chat persistence is client-driven and the /ops-agent demo page never writes; decision: wire it before the next partner demo

- **Trigger:** the evidence probe for CC-2026-10-01-001 found `ops_agent_messages` at ZERO rows in production even though chat persistence shipped 2026-09-27 (CC-2026-09-25-011).
- **Root cause:** persistence is CLIENT-DRIVEN. The server endpoints (server/routes/api.ts:173-176 → server/controllers/ops-chat.ts) only read/append/update/delete when asked. Exactly one client surface writes: the global floating widget `client/src/components/OpsAgentChat.tsx` (hydrates its thread on mount, POSTs every message, PATCHes run outcomes; watchdog reports deliberately skipped). The `/ops-agent` PAGE (`client/src/pages/OpsCleanupAgent.tsx`) — the surface Robert used for the Dheerendar demo — POSTs straight to `/api/orchestrator/chat` and never touches the thread endpoints (no threadId/localStorage/persist references anywhere in the file); CommandCenter's chat shares the same non-persisting shape. Net effect: page-based sessions — i.e., every partner demo — leave zero chat rows, while widget sessions leave rows.
- **Decision (Robert delegated the call):** YES — persist chat transcripts before the next partner-facing demo. Implementation is client-only: wire `OpsCleanupAgent.tsx` to the existing endpoints exactly as the widget already does (thread id in localStorage, hydrate on mount, persist user/assistant messages including proposal cards, PATCH runResult/executionStatus when the run lands). No server changes; manual Cloud Build deploy as usual. Queued as CC-2026-10-01-002 (Fast Lane-eligible).
- **Recorded as:** CC-2026-10-01-002 (detail entry at bottom of register).

## 2026-09-30 (night) — Ops Agent "stuck on a DAG" fixed: Google retired the pinned gemini-2.5-flash; shared model chain + honest fallback shipped

- **Trigger:** Robert's production screenshots: two conversational questions
  ("What time was your last update?", "Have you been updated recently?")
  both returned the IDENTICAL canned 4-step DAG proposal in first person,
  badge reading "Model: urc-model-gemini-fallback | token usage not reported".
- **Root cause (production logs):** AI_APICallError 404 NOT_FOUND — "This
  model models/gemini-2.5-flash is no longer available to new users",
  pointing at the 3.x family. Every call site pinning retired 2.x/1.x ids
  failed silently into per-site fallbacks; the orchestrator chat's catch-all
  synthesized the deterministic template DAG for EVERY failure — including
  purely conversational turns — and spoke as if a real analysis had happened.
  DAG EXECUTION kept working because agent-runner already rode the -latest
  alias chain (09-28) — the exact asymmetry that made the bug look like a
  routing bug rather than a model-availability bug.
- **Fix (23 files):** shared GOOGLE_MODEL_CHAIN (gemini-flash-latest →
  gemini-3.8-flash → gemini-pro-latest) + withGoogleModelChain helper in
  google-ai.ts; the Ops Agent chat rides the chain on BOTH paths and reports
  the model that actually answered; the catch-all now degrades MODE-AWARE and
  HONEST — no proposal object, no fabricated analysis, "Tried: …" naming the
  chain. The public founder-intake chat (whose whole chain was dead ids)
  healed; assessment-questions, icp, artifacts, billing-engine and
  /api/debug/llm moved onto the chain; deterministic-path audit-log labels
  relabeled "not-llm-dispatch" (campaigns ×3, fulfillment, aiStudioSync ×2,
  marketplace); agents.ts seed/default baseModels → current ids; client
  pickers (Ops Agent, Agents, Settings) offer current ids.
- **Bonus repair:** root node_modules was missing the @trpc/react-query
  junction (declared dep, silently absent — "pnpm install" reported "Already
  up to date" without healing). Restored via a targeted add; ~11 phantom
  client tsc errors vanished, returning the typecheck to its documented
  4-error cookie@2 baseline. Manifests unchanged.
- **Verification:** vitest 495/495 (8 new pins: chain order and no-retired-ids,
  honest fallback in both modes, success-path model reporting, source pins);
  tsc at the documented 4-error baseline; live probe
  scripts/probe-gemini-chain.ts — all three chain models return "pong" with
  real token counts on the ADC user-principal credential.
- **Owner:** Buffy agent session, pending Robert review.

## 2026-09-30 (evening) — Main app audit driven to ZERO findings: vestigial firebase + tsoa removed; sub-projects swept

- **Change (deps, 3 files):** GitHub's dashboard showed 260 findings
  (14 critical) on the default branch; investigation showed the real per-
  manifest picture: the MAIN app had 4 findings (2 high), and the rest came
  from sub-project manifests. Remediation:
  (1) `package.json` — removed `firebase` (a declared PROD dependency with
  ZERO imports anywhere in server/ or client/, no firebase.json/.firebaserc,
  no config) and `tsoa` (devDependency with no tsoa.json, no scripts, no
  imports). Consequences: the 2 high findings died with them — path-to-regexp
  <0.1.13 existed ONLY in tsoa's express@4 tree (the documented reason the
  09-25 remediation could not override it), and @grpc/grpc-js rode in via
  firebase; ts-deepmerge (moderate) also rode in via tsoa.
  (2) `pnpm-workspace.yaml` — '@firebase/util' removed from
  onlyBuiltDependencies/allowBuilds; the path-to-regexp NOTE updated to
  record why the override is now gone (tsoa removed, CC-2026-09-30-008).
  (3) lockfile regenerated (single install pass).
  Sub-project sweep: agentlabhs cards/pages/settings — `npm audit fix
  --package-lock-only` each, ALL now 0 findings (brace-expansion highs were
  lockfile-bump-only). pr-posse — reduced; remaining 2 findings (moderate
  @opentelemetry/core <2.8.0 + 1 high in the agentic-flow tree) require a
  BREAKING major bump of agentic-flow to 1.10.2: not forced on a demo lane;
  filed as a dated decision in scheduled-change-queue (CC-2026-09-30-008).
- **Verification:** `pnpm audit` → **"No known vulnerabilities found"**
  (from 4 findings / 2 high; from 35 pre-remediation on 09-25); vitest
  487/487; tsc — the 4 remaining errors are the documented pre-existing
  cookie@2 noise in authRoutes/sdk.ts (untouched by this change; Cloud Build
  does not typecheck); change-control green.
- **AMENDED 2026-09-30 (CC-2026-09-30-009):** the firebase removal was a
  FALSE-POSITIVE diagnosis — Robert confirmed firebase IS used by standalone
  and built-in apps (e.g. `agentlab/package.json` declares it and resolves it
  UPWARD into root node_modules, which is why no root-level import exists).
  Firebase was restored at root the same day with the @grpc/grpc-js high
  killed via a pinned override (>=1.14.5) instead of removal. Audit remains
  clean. tsoa removal stands (no correction). See CC-2026-09-30-009.
- **Deploy note:** dependency change rides the next Cloud Build; the image
  gets smaller and the build simpler.
- **Owner:** Buffy agent session, pending Robert review.

## Purpose

This register stops fast operational changes from leaking past the audit trail.
It is intentionally file-based so it stays free, local, and agent-readable.

Use this when changing:

- kit manifests
- workflow source files
- agent prompts
- tracker schemas
- operating architecture docs
- CRM-lite, finance, or funnel control files

## Rule

Every meaningful change needs one visible entry before the work is considered done.

Small typo fixes can be grouped. Anything that changes behavior, ownership,
workflow steps, automations, source-of-truth status, or required tools needs its
own entry.

## 2026-09-30 (late) — Execution lease + poller implemented (code); Cloud Run Job/Scheduler wiring follows deploy

- **Change (code, 5 files + 1 new):**
  (1) `server/schema.ts` — `workflow_runs.locked_at`/`locked_by` lease columns
  + `idx_workflow_runs_claim` index.
  (2) `server/db.ts` — matching idempotent DDL in `ensureDatabaseSchema()`.
  (3) `server/execution/queue-processor.ts` — `claimPendingRuns()` atomic
  claim (CTE UPDATE + `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), `requeueExpiredLeases()`
  10-min-TTL dead-worker recovery, per-step heartbeat guard
  `locked_by = worker`, `processPendingRuns(limit)` now returns claimed
  count; body mapped from raw snake_case claim rows (only 5 fields used —
  verified by grep). This is the prerequisite correctness guard: prior shape
  selected all pending rows with no claim guard and was only accidentally
  safe while at most one lane ran.
  (4) `server/_core/poll-tick.ts` (new) — Job entrypoint: recovery +
  claim/drain loop, exits when idle, 50-min runaway budget; bundled by the
  build script to `dist/poll-tick.js` (esbuild multi-entry keeps outputs
  flat — Dockerfile CMD untouched).
  (5) `server/_core/index.ts` — `POST /api/internal/poller-kick` registered
  before the `/api` mount (no tenant middleware): auth = Scheduler OIDC
  (Google JWKS, iss accounts.google.com, aud = service URL, email pinned to
  `POLLER_SCHEDULER_SA`) XOR `KICK_SECRET` (constant-time compare);
  fire-and-forget Run API `jobs/{name}:run` with active-execution quota
  guard; answers 503 when unconfigured — honest, never open.
  (6) `package.json` — build script adds the second esbuild entrypoint.
- **Why:** implements `cloud-run-poller-design.md`. Pending runs currently
  sit forever unless an authenticated request path sweeps them (bf87810f
  waited ~26 h across two credential failures).
- **Verification (local):** targeted suite 16/16
  (`orchestrator-execute.test.ts`, `actions/router.test.ts` — the two files
  exercising the execution pipeline); `tsc --noEmit`: ZERO errors in the five
  touched files (full-tree noise is pre-existing: stale `cookie` types since
  the cookie@2 merge — Cloud Build never typechecks, and an incomplete
  `lucide-react` store entry from the interrupted install — build-time only,
  self-heals on next clean install).
- **Deployment sequence (next, same change):** push → Cloud Build revision
  Ready → Secret Manager `agentlab-gemini-key` + `agentlab-poller-kick`
  (+ Secret Accessor on the runtime SA) → service `--update-secrets` →
  Job `agentlab-poller` (same image, command `node dist/poll-tick.js`, full
  env minus PORT) → grant `roles/run.jobs.run` to the service runtime SA →
  Scheduler `agentlab-poller-kick` (1 min, OIDC to the endpoint) → E2E:
  seed pending run → ≤2 min → verify claim/lock/evidence.
- **Rollback:** pause Scheduler job (service unaffected); revert = previous
  revision; the lease columns are inert to old code paths.

## 2026-09-30 (small hours) — Poller design + key-rotation runbook authored

- **Change:** Added two operational documents:
  `docs/operations/cloud-run-poller-design.md` (proposal) and
  `docs/operations/ai-studio-key-rotation-runbook.md` (active runbook).
- **Why:** The 09-29 night entry proved pending runs never execute in the
  background on Cloud Run; today's sweep-vehicle workaround worked but is
  manual. The design doc specifies the fix: Cloud Run **Job** (`agentlab-poller`)
  kicked every minute by Cloud Scheduler through an authenticated service
  endpoint, with a `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` DB lease + 10-min heartbeat/TTL on
  `workflow_runs` as the prerequisite correctness guard — the processor
  currently selects all pending runs with no claim guard, so any concurrent
  poller would double-execute steps. Rejected alternatives documented with
  reasons (in-service setInterval starves/duplicates; HTTP Scheduler lane
  hits the 60-min request ceiling vs 7–52 min observed runs). The runbook
  encodes tonight's exposure: the live key crossed chat + Cloud Run CLI +
  local env, so it is condemned pending rotation; runbook fixes the safe
  ordering (new key verified in production BEFORE old key deleted), covers
  service + Job + local `.env.local` sync, and the cookie@2 non-Ready-revision
  rollback trap.
- **No code changed.** Both docs await Robert's go-ahead (implementation is a
  separate scheduled change; rotation is Robert's manual step 1 in AI Studio).

## 2026-09-29 (evening) — cookie@2 boot-crash fixed; morning zombie-instance theory corrected

- **Change:** `server/_core/authRoutes.ts` and `server/_core/sdk.ts`: import
  renamed from `parse` to `parseCookie` (cookie@2's new API, Dependabot #81
  merged 1.1.1 → 2.0.1). The local alias `parseCookieHeader` is preserved, so
  no call sites changed. These are the only two files importing `cookie`.
- **Why:** All three post-merge deploys (revisions 00176–00178, PRs #81/#82/#83,
  19:40–19:52Z) crashed on boot:
  `SyntaxError: The requested module 'cookie' does not provide an export named 'parse'`
  → `HealthCheckContainerError`. Production traffic stayed on the last-good
  revision 00175 (uuid bump, PR #80), so no downtime — but main was
  undeployable and every new merge kept producing dead revisions.
- **CORRECTION of this morning's entry:** the "stale pre-09-27 Cloud Run
  instance" theory is wrong — no zombie executor exists. The real mechanics:
  deploys trail pushes (yesterday's model-chain fix deployed 05:14 local), the
  always-on Cloud Run instance processes pending runs continuously, and the
  15:42Z `bf87810f` failure used the NEW chain (`gemini-pro-latest`) because
  the new code was already live — the quota exhaustion is REAL free-tier
  exhaustion on the service-account credential (which also explains how
  executors get model errors at all while the stored API key 401s). The
  04:03Z `f28b9301` failure (`gemini-2.5-pro`, ops_agent_execute trigger) was
  revision 00175's predecessor code — old chain by deploy timing, not a rogue
  process.
- **Verified:** cookie@2.0.1 exports confirmed from unpkg (`parseCookie`,
  `stringifyCookie`; no `parse`). Repo-wide import scan: exactly two importers,
  both fixed. Local suite/typecheck COULD NOT run: `pnpm install` hangs on this
  machine (two attempts, 10-minute timeouts, even with CI=true) and
  node_modules is half-unlinked (cookie unresolvable) — local verification is
  blocked until install is repaired; do NOT restart the local dev server until
  then. Verification path instead: push → Cloud Build fresh install → revision
  Ready check; worst case equals the current safe state (traffic pinned to
  00175).
- **State at close (verified):** fix pushed as `7bcef2ae`; Cloud Build
  `083fbb85` SUCCESS; revision `agentlab-00179-wm9` Ready (first Ready since
  00175); production smoke HTTP 200 in 0.35s. Deploy lane restored. Open
  blockers unchanged: Gemini free-tier quota exhausted (all agents fail until
  a paid tier or fresh credential), P1 scopes upload with HubSpot support,
  local install repair queued (dev server down until then).
- **Owner:** Agent session, approved by Robert (in-chat; deploys on push are
  the standing instruction this session).

## 2026-09-29 — P2 step 1 done: thin dispatch rejected; stale remote executor + dead model chain exposed

- **Change:** (1) Extended `scripts/dispatch-approved-action.ts` with a `--reject
  <dispatchId> "<reason>"` mode. It does NOT mirror the router's reject
  semantics verbatim: the queue processor skips any step with a `completed`
  run-step row, so the script marks the step row `rejected` (reason in
  `error_message` + `output_payload`) instead — a resume re-executes the step
  and inserts a fresh row, preserving history. It resets the run to `pending`
  only when the run is `paused_for_approval`; any other state is left untouched
  with an explicit message. (2) `scripts/probe-run-context.mjs` now resolves
  short-ID prefixes (`bf87810f`) to full UUIDs, matching the register's ID
  convention. (3) Executed the rejection of dispatch `ed7f0890` with reason:
  "no email connector existed at draft time; superseded by
  hubspot_marketing_email; re-draft required".
- **Why:** Morning handoff P2 step 1. The thin duplicate dispatch would have
  overwritten Sandra Hill's rich CRM record ("Follow up Thurs" notes vs the
  full approved payload). Rejection removes the accidental-approval risk
  permanently, including from any stale remote UI.
- **Verified:** Read-only probe first: `ed7f0890` was `awaiting_approval` with
  the thin payload; dispatch `2cd0f477` (the good one) is `dispatched` and
  untouched. Post-reject probe: `ed7f0890` = `rejected` with the recorded
  reason; step row marked `rejected`; run row untouched (see below).
- **Incident discovered during probe (stale-code class, new instance):** Run
  `bf87810f` is now `failed` (was `paused_for_approval` at last night's close).
  At 2026-09-29T15:42Z, step 6 was re-attempted and failed with the model chain
  names `gemini-pro-latest` (quota exhausted) and `gemini-2.5-pro`
  ("no longer available to new users") — `gemini-2.5-pro` is not in the current
  chain (`gemini-flash-latest`, `gemini-3.8-flash`, `gemini-pro-latest`, shipped

  Cloud Run instance is still executing pending runs from the shared production

  run reset to `pending` can be grabbed by stale code before a local execution.

## 2026-09-28 — handoff doc: ADC re-login paste warning

- **Change:** Added a one-line paste warning to the Priority 1 gcloud command in
  `docs/operations/morning-handoff-2026-09-28.md` (no command text altered).
- **Why:** Robert's paste of the valid command produced a misleading
  `--scopes` validation error; SDK source (surface/auth/application_default/login.py:272,
  SDK 584.0.0) shows an exact-string check, and a byte-level check of the doc
  confirmed the stored command is clean. Rich-text/terminal paste artifacts are
  the likely cause.
- **Verified:** Controlled re-run of the exact command with `--no-launch-browser`
  passed scope validation and built the consent URL with both scopes.
- **Owner:** Agent session, approved by Robert (in-chat).
- **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-28:** This entry blamed paste artifacts; wrong. See the
  follow-up entry below.

## 2026-09-28 — handoff doc: quote --scopes in PowerShell (root cause)

- **Change:** Priority 1 gcloud command in `morning-handoff-2026-09-28.md` now
  quotes the scopes value:
  `--scopes="https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform,https://www.googleapis.com/auth/generative-language"`.
  The paste-warning note was replaced with the verified root cause.
- **Why:** Robert reproduced the error twice from PowerShell in the repo folder.
  PowerShell parses an unquoted comma as an array separator; the array is then
  flattened with the comma replaced by a space. gcloud's debug log shows it
  received one mangled scope (`cloud-platform generative-language` as a single
  token), which fails the exact-string `cloud-platform` membership check at
  surface/auth/application_default/login.py:272. Bash/cmd wrappers pass the
  value through intact, which is why the earlier Git Bash test passed.
- **Verified:** The same PowerShell invocation with the scopes value quoted
  passed validation and built the correct consent URL. gcloud debug log
  (`%APPDATA%\gcloud\logs`) captured the mangled argument. Temp shim/scripts
  removed; only the two doc changes remain.
- **Owner:** Agent session, approved by Robert (in-chat).

## 2026-09-28 — handoff doc: Gemini consent scope is generative-language.retriever

- **Change:** Priority 1 gcloud command in `morning-handoff-2026-09-28.md` now
  requests `--scopes="https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform,https://www.googleapis.com/auth/generative-language.retriever"`.
- **Why:** After the quoting fix, Google's consent screen rejected bare
  `generative-language` with `Error 400: invalid_scope` ("Some requested scopes
  cannot be shown"). Google's official Gemini OAuth quickstart
  (ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/oauth, Aug 2026) documents the ADC scope for
  this API as `generative-language.retriever`. This matters end to end because
  the app's ADC mint path (`server/_core/google-ai.ts`) requests no scope at
  refresh time — the consented set from the login is what lands on every token,
  so the login scope choice is the only one that counts.
- **Open question:** Unverified whether the Gemini API accepts `.retriever`
  tokens on this gateway (probe prints `SUCCESS` only if it does). The API may
  also accept `cloud-platform` tokens for user principals; Robert's next probe
  run will tell us without another login if that is true.
- **Owner:** Agent session, approved by Robert (in-chat).

- **Why:** Robert hit "This app is blocked" consenting on Google's default SDK
  client, then `org_internal` ("restricted to users within its organization")
  on the new client — root cause was the project's Internal audience. Fix
  follows Google's Gemini OAuth quickstart (own Desktop client, External
  Testing audience, self as test user). Google no longer allows downloading
  existing client secrets, hence the new client. The existing "Portable Founder
  Dashboard" client was left untouched.

  before the audience change and reached the normal account chooser after it.
  Note: the Gemini API previously rejected a `cloud-platform`-only token
  (`ACCESS_TOKEN_SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT`), so the `.retriever` consent is required.
  Final gate is the live probe: `node scripts/probe-adc-scope.mjs` must print
  `SUCCESS` after Robert completes the login.
- **Owner:** Agent session, approved by Robert (in-chat).

  `scripts/dispatch-approved-action.ts` executes an approved dispatch through
  the exact actions-router path. Suite 476/476.

  connector forwarded them raw (would 400 at HubSpot). Root cause of the
  whole gap: the blueprint (Sep 23) was committed but its portal properties
  were never provisioned, and the stored PAT returns **403 (missing schema
  scopes)** on every property/group creation — so even the provisioner cannot
  finish without a HubSpot scope grant.
- **State:** Dispatch `2cd0f477` recorded `dispatch_failed` with HubSpot's
  real 400 (`agentlab_intake_summary` does not exist). Nothing was written to
  the production CRM. Robert approved this dispatch three times in-session
  (twice consumed by the run-level endpoint on the pre-09-27 Cloud Run
  deploy; once at the card with no dispatch wiring in the deployed UI).
- **Needed from Robert:** grant `crm.schemas.contacts.write` (+ groups) to the
  HubSpot private app, then agent re-runs provisioner → dispatch. Alternative:
  approve a reduced identity-only write (approved signal stays in the OS
  evidence, not the CRM).
- **Owner:** Agent session, approved by Robert (in-chat).

## 2026-09-28 — GUARDRAIL: HubSpot two-portal map (after near-miss)

- **Map (probe-verified):** The OS's HubSpot credentials belong to portal
  **243478405** (Marketing Hub Enterprise, app `agentlabhs` / private app id
  53416564, UI host `app-na2.hubspot.com`). This is where all OS writes land,

  has TWO legitimate targets and one forbidden one. **`--account 52008786`
  (newdevtest) is where the agentlabhs project/app was built** — project
  uploads (`hs project upload`) belong there; installed framework id
  52709753. **`--account 243478405` is production** — all data/API operations

  243 private-app entry (53416564) is the production INSTALL of the 52008786
  project app; its scopes update when a new build from 52008786 is promoted
  to the 243 installation, not via the Auth screen.

  `https://app-na2.hubspot.com/l/personal-access-key/243478405`) — NOT a
  `pat-…` private-app token (the CLI rejects PATs with 400; both credential
  species verified empirically). PATs belong in the OS/Infisical, the PAK in
  the CLI config.

## Log Distribution Rule

Station a log beside the workflow, kit, tracker, or automation it verifies when
that local placement helps someone run or debug the work.

If the log is a compliance, audit, certification, readiness, or cross-workflow
evidence record and does not need to live beside a specific artifact, place it
in:

`C:\Users\thebo\OneDrive - Uncle Robert Consulting LLC\Working Docs\AI Native Agency Deepened\Compliance Audits`

That folder is the temporary compliance-audit home until it is promoted into
the Operations SOP structure.

## Change Scheduling

Use scheduled changes whenever possible. This is not meant to slow the work
down; it prevents five good ideas from firing at once and hiding the one that
breaks the system.

Scheduled changes live in `docs/operations/scheduled-change-queue.md`.

Use the fast lane only when a change is needed to unblock testing,
certification, packaging, or market release. Fast-lane changes still need a
change-control entry before the session ends.

## Required Entry Fields

| Field                  | Meaning                                                             |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Date                   | YYYY-MM-DD                                                          |
| Change ID              | Stable ID such as `CC-2026-05-07-001`                               |
| Area                   | Kit, Finance, CRM-lite, Book Funnel, Event, Agent, Automation, Site |
| Files                  | Main files changed                                                  |
| Summary                | What changed in plain English                                       |
| Reason                 | Why the change was needed                                           |
| Source of Truth Impact | New, updated, deprecated, or none                                   |
| Automation Impact      | n8n, script, manual, none                                           |
| Reviewer               | Person or agent that checked it                                     |
| Status                 | Proposed, Active, Superseded, Reverted                              |

## Completion Checklist

Before ending a change session:

- [ ] Add or update the relevant changelog entry.
- [ ] If an agent prompt changed, update `assets/agent-versions.md`.
- [ ] If a kit changed, update `changeLog` frontmatter and `## Change Log`.
- [ ] Put compliance/audit/certification logs in the Compliance Audits folder unless they must stay local to the artifact.
- [ ] Move non-urgent work into `docs/operations/scheduled-change-queue.md`.
- [ ] Run `pnpm change-control:check`.
- [ ] Report changed files and any failed checks.

## CC-2026-09-23-021 — Tier 1 runner hardening: step policy, cancellation, artifact export
- **Date**: 2026-09-23
- **Scope**: server/schema.ts, drizzle/0010-runner-hardening.sql, server/db.ts, server/execution/step-policy.ts (new), server/execution/queue-processor.ts, server/controllers/runs.ts, server/controllers/export.ts (new), server/routes/api.ts, server/execution/step-policy.test.ts, server/controllers/export.test.ts, docs
- **Change**: Implemented conversion-plan Tier 1 items 1–2. (a) Per-step policy: `workflow_steps.timeout_seconds` (default 300, clamped 30–1800) and `max_retries` (default 1, clamped 0–3) via migration 0010 + self-heal DDL; `runWithStepPolicy` enforces timeout via AbortController, retries only on timeout/error (never on model refusals, which are honest failures), and checks `cancel_requested` between attempts. (b) Cancellation: `POST /api/runs/:runId/cancel` sets `cancel_requested` + `cancelled_at` on running/pending runs (terminal-status runs rejected honestly); the queue processor honors it between steps and inside step attempts (StepCancelledError → runStep + run marked `cancelled`, not `failed`). (c) Export: `GET /api/runs/:runId/export` (Markdown bundle: manifest, per-step transcripts, artifacts with grades) and `GET /api/artifacts/:artifactId/export` (single artifact), with ownership checks and honest 404s.
- **Tests**: 12 new hermetic tests (policy bounds/clamps/retry/cancel, export shape/headers/ownership); suite 389/389, typecheck 0.
- **Rollback**: Revert schema + drop the three added columns; endpoints are additive.

## CC-2026-09-23-022 — Tier 1 client-facing runs view: shareable honesty console
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Scope**: server/schema.ts, drizzle/0011-workflow-share-tokens.sql, drizzle/meta/_journal.json, server/db.ts, server/controllers/share.ts, server/controllers/share.test.ts, server/routes/api.ts, client/src/pages/RunConsole.tsx, client/src/pages/SharedRuns.tsx, client/src/App.tsx, docs
- **Change**: Implemented conversion-plan Tier 1 item 3. (a) `workflow_share_tokens` table (migration 0011 + self-heal DDL): tokens stored as sha256 hashes with a unique index, scope 'all'|'single', revocation as a timestamp so the audit trail survives. (b) Operator endpoints: `GET/POST /api/share/tokens`, `DELETE /api/share/tokens/:tokenId` (tenant-scoped; single-run scope verifies run ownership; the raw token is returned exactly once and the hash never leaves the server). (c) Public token-scoped read-only endpoints: `GET /api/share/runs` and `/api/share/runs/:runId` resolve the workspace from the token hash (never req.workspaceId, which the tenant middleware sentinel-fills for unauthenticated requests) and return explicit field projections only — no workspaceId, initialContext, or step input/output payloads; totals are real sums over stored rows; runs view touches lastAccessedAt best-effort. (d) Client: `/run-console` (operator: create/copy/revoke links, see last-opened; preview of fresh token shown once) and `/shared/runs?token=…` (public: real status badges, per-step cost/latency, artifacts with grades or honest 'not evaluated', 15s auto-refresh). Tests caught a real bug: creation hashed the prefixed token while lookup hashed the bare value — unified to hash the bare value so links work with or without the prefix.
- **Tests**: 8 new hermetic tests with a condition-evaluating drizzle mock that honors select projections (leakage assertions are meaningful, not vacuous); suite 397/397, typecheck 0.
- **Rollback**: Revert schema + drop workflow_share_tokens; routes and pages are additive.

## CC-2026-09-23-023 — Hotfix / ClientMessenger / Production render crash on /messages
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Type**: security-adjacent production fix (availability)
- **Scope**: client/src/pages/ClientMessenger.tsx
- **Change**: Production /messages crashed with "TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'id')" after the DB-backed messenger rewire. Root cause: the getMessages useQuery input asserted `activeThread!.id` at render time — evaluated on every render even though the query is disabled while threads load. The pre-rewire page sourced threads synchronously from localStorage (never empty on first paint), masking the pattern; with async tRPC data the first render has `threads === []`, so the assertion threw before any guard ran. Fixed by passing `activeThread?.id ?? ""` with the existing `enabled: !!activeThread` gate. Also fixed a sibling latent crash: `activeThread?.name.charAt(0)` (optional-chained the object but not the possibly-null name) → `activeThread?.name?.charAt(0)`. Audited all touched pages for other render-time non-null assertions: none remain.
- **Tests**: Full suite 397/397, typecheck 0 (regression covered by the honest-render doctrine; page-level crash class documented here).
- **Rollback**: Revert the two-line change.

## CC-2026-09-24-001 — Ops / Deploy pipeline / GitHub Actions broken since 2026-09-16; manual deploy path documented
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Type**: operational (CI/CD availability)
- **Scope**: .github/workflows/deploy.yml (unchanged, finding only), production Cloud Run service
- **Change**: While shipping CC-023, discovered the "Build and Deploy to Artifact Registry" GitHub Actions workflow has FAILED on every push since 2026-09-16: the google-github-actions/auth step errors because the GCP_SA_KEY secret is missing/empty in the repo. Production therefore never received automated deploys; the 2026-09-23 bundle reached Cloud Run through another (manual/other) path. Deployed CC-022 + CC-023 to production via the documented manual path: `gcloud builds submit --config cloudbuild.yaml --substitutions COMMIT_SHA=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD) .` (the COMMIT_SHA substitution is REQUIRED for manual submits - without it the docker -t tag is invalid and build step 0 exits 125). Verified new bundle live, /api/share/runs honest 401, /messages 200.
- **Action for Robert (one step)**: add the `GCP_SA_KEY` repository secret (a service-account JSON key with Cloud Run + Artifact Registry + Cloud Build roles) to restore push-to-deploy, or delete deploy.yml to make the manual path canonical. Until then, deploys require the manual command above.
- **Rollback**: n/a (documentation + one production deploy of already-reviewed code)

## CC-2026-09-24-002 — Ops / Dashboard & Settings / Integration launcher rewired to real targets (dedupe + n8n VM)
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Type**: fix (1-click launcher correctness)
- **Scope**: client/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx; client/src/pages/Settings.tsx
- **Change**: The dashboard "Connected Integrations & 1-Click Assets" launcher showed duplicate cards: vault-synced DB rows (Settings secret sync auto-creates `HubSpot CRM`, `Instantly.ai Outbound Engine`, etc. in workspace_integrations) were appended after the canonical list with no dedupe — one HubSpot card went to HubSpot, the DB copy fell back to Settings. Fixed by filtering DB rows whose name matches a canonical integration (MCP rows excluded as before); user-added custom portal cards still appear. Rewired wrong targets: n8n now points at the self-hosted GCloud e2-micro VM (`http://35.225.47.185:5678`, VM `my-micro-vm`, firewall rules `allow-agent-lab-ports`/`allow-n8n` on 5678/5679) instead of https://n8n.io; overridable via `VITE_N8N_URL`. AgentMail now opens the Client Messenger (`/messages`, where AgentMail ingestion lands) instead of /command-center. M365 card points at `https://m365.cloud.microsoft/apps` instead of the deprecated portal.office.com redirect. Pulse Social and Market Marksman point at their hosted homes (Vercel / Cloud Run, matching Pricing.tsx buttons) instead of localhost dev servers — overridable via `VITE_PULSE_SOCIAL_URL` / `VITE_MARKSMAN_URL`. Settings' "n8n Autonomous Workflow Trigger" webhook preset repointed from localhost:5678 to the VM; n8n core-integration card details now record the VM URL.
- **Reason**: Robert reported the launcher cards were wonky — two HubSpot cards (one dead-end to command center fallback) and two n8n cards both hitting the local CLI instance he wants to sleep at night; n8n was migrated to a GCloud VM that the UI never learned about.
- **Tests**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Click-target verification pending a dev-server smoke pass; DB row names matched case-insensitively ("HubSpot CRM" filtered).
- **Follow-up**: if the VM URL changes (external IPs are not static on e2-micro), set `VITE_N8N_URL` or a reserved IP; the webhook URL used by `N8N_INTAKE_WEBHOOK_URL` (server-side intake relay) is a separate env var and may also need the VM address.
- **Rollback**: Revert the two client files; no schema or API changes.

## CC-2026-09-24-003 — Fullstack / Tenant Isolation, Orchestrator Voice, Visitor Memory, DAG Failure Diagnosis
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Type**: security + behavior fix (four-part rewiring per Robert's approval)
- **Scope**: server/_core/env.ts; server/_core/authRoutes.ts; server/settings/router.ts; server/execution/agent-runner.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator.ts; server/founder-intake/router.ts; server/schema.ts; server/db.ts; client/src/components/OpsAgentChat.tsx
- **1. Multi-tenant credential isolation (SECURITY)**: `syncWorkspaceVaultSecrets` previously bridged the operator's process.env keys (HUBSPOT_PAT, INSTANTLY_API_KEY, ELEVENLABS_API_KEY, GEMINI) into the FIRST 10 workspace rows on boot and into ANY workspace that opened Settings; `agent-runner` read those env keys regardless of workspace, so any new tenant's runs could execute against Robert's live HubSpot/Instantly accounts, and a tenant saving a secret in Settings would overwrite the operator's live process.env AND `.env.local`. Fixed: vault sync now writes operator env keys only into operator workspaces (`isOperatorWorkspace`: OPERATOR_WORKSPACE_ID/DEFAULT_WORKSPACE_ID env or canonical ...0001); non-operator workspaces get stale connected rows flipped disconnected; `upsertSecret` applies to shared env/.env.local only for the operator workspace; `resolveWorkspaceCredentials()` resolves HubSpot/Instantly tokens per workspace (operator env for operator, empty for others) and the Instantly tools fail honestly with INSTANTLY_NOT_CONFIGURED_FOR_WORKSPACE instead of silently using operator credentials. HubSpot tools were already token-gated and now inherit the per-workspace token.
- **2. Orchestrator conversational mode**: `handleOrchestratorChat` previously forced EVERY response through generateObject(workflowProposalSchema) — structurally unable to converse, diagnose, or answer; also single-turn (no history). Fixed: mode routing (proposal only when the request is build/create/automate/execute-shaped or forceProposal=true; otherwise generateText consultative dialogue grounded in telemetry), conversation history (last 12 turns) accepted and passed through, telemetry now includes real FAILED RUN error messages (recentRunFailures), and the client sends history. OpsAgentChat catch handler no longer shows a canned "processed successfully" on API failure; "Synthesizing multi-agent DAG..." spinner renamed to "Thinking...".
- **3. Visitor memory → account handoff**: new `visitor_profiles` table (schema + ensureDatabaseSchema bootstrap; unique on email). Founder intake `respond` now persists name/company/painPoint/interest + bounded rolling transcript (12 turns, 8KB) per turn once an email is shared — previously context lived only in React state and died with the tab. On signup, `claimVisitorProfile` consumes the profile and seeds the workspace (name + new `workspaces.onboarding_context` jsonb column). Orchestrator telemetry surfaces prospectContext so the Ops Agent greets returning prospects with their stated context. Playbooks/workflows/apps remain canonical (not per-workspace copied) — the workspace is empty of user data/integrations/secrets as designed, except what the visitor actually told us.
- **4. DAG instant-failure diagnosis (evidence, not guesswork)**: queried workflow_runs/workflow_run_steps/audit_logs directly. Failure histogram: 5x "Failed after 3 attempts: You exceeded your current quota" (GEMINI quota exhaustion), 2x "models/gemini-1.5-pro is not found for API version v1beta" (dead model in fallback chain), 6x "API key not valid", plus legitimate Refused-by-user pauses and 2x refusal-tripwire kills. Fixes applied: agent-runner fallback chain gemini-1.5-flash/gemini-1.5-pro → gemini-2.0-flash/gemini-2.5-pro (1.5 models are dead for this key; steps burned retries on models that could never succeed). A planned refusal-tripwire loosening was attempted and REVERTED after autonomous-execution.test.ts correctly failed: passive-acknowledgment phrases encode a deliberate non-execution policy. The real killers were infrastructure, now fixed at source. NOTE for Robert: the 6 "API key not valid" failures and quota exhaustion are YOUR env keys — check the GEMINI key in .env.local; the ops agent will now see and report these errors itself.

## CC-2026-09-24-004 — Fullstack / Smoke test / Schema bootstrap fixed + two latent bugs found, env gaps surfaced
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Type**: fix + smoke-test findings (four-behavior end-to-end verification)
- **Scope**: server/db.ts; server/_core/index.ts; server/_core/authRoutes.ts; server/founder-intake/visitor-memory.test.ts (new)
- **Smoke test findings & fixes**:
  1. **ensureDatabaseSchema silently dead since inception (FIXED)**: every boot, the bootstrap died on its first multi-statement DDL block with 'cannot insert multiple commands into a prepared statement' (Neon pooler runs PgBouncer transaction mode; the extended-protocol prepared statement path rejects multi-command templates). Because the catch only warned, ALL subsequent schema self-healing and vault sync silently no-oped every boot. Fixed: postgres.js client now `{ prepare: false }` (PgBouncer-safe unnamed statements) and the DDL templates run through a `.simple()`-protocol wrapper (simple query protocol supports multi-command statements). Verified: boot log now shows 'Schema bootstrap OK (attempt 1)' and visitor_profiles/onboarding_context materialize.
  2. **Signup silently bound new users to the OPERATOR workspace when DB writes failed (FIXED)**: on DB failure the signup handler fell back to an in-memory user with workspaceId ...0001 (operator). Live-verified: a probe signup 'succeeded' and /api/auth/me returned workspaceId ...0001 for a stranger. Fixed: signup now returns 503 honestly when persistence fails; the in-memory fallback (and its operator-workspace binding) is removed from the signup path.
  3. **DB connectivity flap (ENV, unfixed — Robert action)**: the long-lived server process gets intermittent ECONNREFUSED to the Neon pooler on new pool connections (both families; fresh processes connect fine; ~1 in 3 requests affected during testing). Likely free-tier compute suspend/wake or pooler limits — possibly aggravated by the second stale dev server on port 3000 holding pool connections. Recommend: check Neon compute status/connection count; consider direct (non-pooler) string for migrations only; keep boot retry. IPv4-first (dns.setDefaultResultOrder) and connect_timeout 15 added on evidence; speculative network options tried and reverted.
  4. **No LLM keys in env (ENV, Robert action)**: GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY / GEMINI_API_KEY / OPENAI_API_KEY are ALL absent from merged env. This is the upstream cause of the ops agent's canned fallback replies, the intake agent's scripted (non-LLM) conversation, and the historical 'API key not valid' + quota run failures. Until a valid Gemini key is restored, every LLM surface runs on deterministic fallbacks.
- **Smoke results**: Test 1 (isolation) verified: probe signup under DB flap correctly 503s (no more operator binding); with healthy pool the per-workspace guards hold by construction + unit tests. Test 2 (orchestrator modes): mode routing + fallback behavior verified live (fallback engine answers/entitlement-gates correctly); live LLM dialogue blocked by missing env key. Test 3 (visitor memory): intake responds correctly; row persistence blocked by the DB flap at request time — logic covered by new repo-convention unit tests (2 passed), live-path verification pending healthy Neon connectivity. Test 4 (DAG real failure): executor already reports real errors (historical runs show genuine error messages in workflow_runs.errorMessage); the same flap masks fresh live runs.
- **Tests**: full suite 399/399 (new visitor-memory tests), tsc clean.
- **Action for Robert**: (1) restore a valid Gemini API key in .env.local; (2) investigate Neon free-tier compute/pooler status; (3) consider retiring the stale port-3000 dev server (it is an older build without /api/auth routes and holds Neon pool connections).
- **Rollback**: revert server/db.ts + server/_core/index.ts + authRoutes signup guard; remove the new test file.

## CC-2026-09-24-006 — Fullstack / Google Gemini service-account auth (org no-API-key policy) + Neon keepalive option
- **Date**: 2026-09-24
- **Type**: feature + env-compliance (Robert's two remaining tasks: service-key auth, Neon suspend mitigation)
- **Scope**: server/_core/google-ai.ts (new); server/_core/google-ai.test.ts (new); server/execution/agent-runner.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator.ts; server/controllers/icp.ts; server/controllers/assessment-questions.ts; server/controllers/artifacts.ts; server/controllers/image-generation.ts; server/execution/billing-engine.ts; server/routes/api.ts (/debug/llm); server/db.ts; server/_core/index.ts; scripts/verify-gemini-service-account.mjs (new); .gitignore; .env.example (new)
- **Org-policy compliance**: API keys are forbidden by Robert's GCP org. All Gemini calls now authenticate via the service account (ais-gemini-key-c716673060c044c@718497644379.iam.gserviceaccount.com, roles/mcp.toolUser at project level) using the OAuth2 JWT-bearer grant (RFC 7523), RS256-signed with Node's built-in crypto — zero new dependencies. Tokens are cached and refreshed 5 min before expiry. The @ai-sdk/google provider is wrapped with a custom fetch that injects Authorization: Bearer and strips x-goog-api-key. Eight call sites wired through one factory: agent-runner, orchestrator, icp, assessment-questions, artifacts-refine, imagen-3, billing-engine, debug/llm. API-key path preserved only as legacy fallback (SA wins when present).
- **Key handling (SECURITY)**: JSON key NEVER committed — secrets/ added to .gitignore. Resolution order: GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON (inline) → secrets/gemini-service-account.json (file, or GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_FILE override). 11 offline unit tests pin resolution order, JWT claims/signature, header contract (Bearer injected, x-goog-api-key stripped), and cache behavior.
- **Vertex escape hatch**: if the Generative Language API is not enabled on the project, set GOOGLE_AI_BASE_URL to the Vertex prefix (URL shape maps 1:1 onto the SDK's {baseURL}/{modelId}:generateContent with publishers/google/models/... ids); scope auto-switches to cloud-platform.
- **Neon keepalive (opt-in)**: free-plan compute auto-suspend (ENDPOINT INACTIVE bands confirmed in Robert's console screenshot) causes multi-second cold starts and intermittent ECONNREFUSED. NEON_KEEPALIVE_MINUTES=5 pings SELECT 1 on an interval (0.25 CU × 16h ≈ 4 CU-hrs/day ≈ 120/month against the ~190 free allotment — documented honest tradeoff in server/db.ts). Default OFF (pure scale-to-zero).
- **Pending Robert action**: save the service-account JSON key as secrets/gemini-service-account.json, then run: npx tsx scripts/verify-gemini-service-account.mjs — it prints PASS/FAIL per step with the exact IAM/API-enablement fix on failure (no secret material is ever printed). If it reports 403: enable Generative Language API on project-36330a6c-5e91-4901-9dd, or grant roles/aiplatform.user and use the Vertex GOOGLE_AI_BASE_URL.
- **Tests**: full suite 416/416 (11 new google-ai auth tests), tsc clean. Live end-to-end verification pending the key file.
- **Rollback**: revert the listed files; delete google-ai.ts, google-ai.test.ts, verify-gemini-service-account.mjs, .env.example; remove secrets/ from .gitignore.

## CC-2026-09-25-005 - Infisical / Secrets delivery / Move credentials off the laptop disk
- **Date**: 2026-09-25
- **Type**: security infrastructure + secret-hygiene (Robert-approved Infisical migration procedure, executed for this repo)
- **Scope**: server/_core/secrets-source.ts (new); server/_core/secrets-source.test.ts (new); scripts/verify-secrets-source.mjs (new); docs/operations/infisical-secrets-runbook.md (new); server/_core/env.ts; scripts/verify-secrets-bridge.mjs; package.json; .env.example; AGENTS.md; docs/operations/secret-handling-standard.md
- **Why**: credentials lived in `.env` / `.env.local` on one laptop. A central store gives continuity if the machine dies, one rotation surface, and removes the "somebody must hand-edit a secrets file" step that stalled the Gemini wiring (CC-2026-09-25-001/-002/-004).
- **Loader contract**: env.ts no longer loads `.env.local` with `override: true` unconditionally. `loadSecretLayers()` makes precedence explicit: `SECRETS_SOURCE=infisical` (set by the wrapped scripts) or a present `INFISICAL_PROJECT_ID` means the Infisical-injected values win and disk files only fill gaps; otherwise the previous behavior is preserved verbatim. The boot log prints which source was used, plus the names (never values) of keys still filled from disk - that list is the migration to-do list.
- **Write-back guard**: `persistSecretToEnvFile` now no-ops with a warning under Infisical instead of re-creating `.env.local` from the Settings vault, so saving a credential in the UI cannot quietly undo the migration.
- **Commands**: `pnpm dev` runs through `infisical run --env=dev`; `pnpm dev:local` is the documented offline escape hatch (the connection here can be cut - a deliberate choice, not an oversight); `pnpm start:infisical`; `pnpm secrets:check` / `pnpm secrets:check:infisical` report key names, presence and length only, never values.
- **Not done, deliberately scheduled**: CI/CD and Cloud Run machine identities. `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` and the Cloud Run service are the only production path and were left untouched; the runbook records the GCP-id-token / Universal-Auth route plus adjacent findings (GCP_SA_KEY is still a service-account key; the Docker build declares `VITE_*` build args that deploy.yml never passes).
- **Leak status**: git history checked - no `.env`, `.env.local`, or `secrets/` file was ever committed. Outstanding from before: rotate the Neon password exposed by the deleted `test-db.mjs` (CC-2026-09-24-007).
- **Tests**: 6 new hermetic tests (Infisical vs disk precedence, gap filling, disk-mode regression, empty-tree no-op); suite and `tsc --noEmit` re-run clean.
- **Addendum (2026-09-25, same day)**: live migration executed with Robert - Infisical went 3 -> 30 secrets via the paste-import route (drag-drop opens the file in a browser tab instead of uploading; documented). Loader verified in both modes. Removed the dead OAUTH_SERVER_URL path (server/_core/sdk.ts boot-time error + axios baseURL, server/_core/env.ts getter) - a hosted-portal leftover this deployment never uses; sign-in goes through the native auth routes. 7 remaining disk keys (DATABASE_URL, Stripe x3, VITE_FIREBASE x3) live in .env, not .env.local - Robert to paste the second file. tsc clean; auth + secrets-source tests 10/10.
- **Rollback**: revert server/_core/env.ts and package.json; delete secrets-source.ts, secrets-source.test.ts, verify-secrets-source.mjs, infisical-secrets-runbook.md; restore `.env`/`.env.local` from the `.backup` copies; drop the AGENTS.md bullet and the secret-handling-standard mention.
## CC-2026-09-25-006 - Dependencies / Security remediation / pnpm audit 35 -> 2 via targeted overrides
- **Date**: 2026-09-25
- **Type**: security hygiene (dependency remediation, no application code changed)
- **Scope**: pnpm-workspace.yaml (overrides block); pnpm-lock.yaml
- **Finding**: GitHub reported 250 Dependabot alerts (12 critical, 113 high); `pnpm audit` on the real install tree showed 35 findings (0 critical, 10 high, 20 moderate, 5 low), 15 unique. Root cause of the discrepancy: GitHub counts dev-chain paths and per-path duplicates.
- **Fix**: 8 new version overrides + 2 bumped pins in pnpm-workspace.yaml: fast-uri ^3.1.4, hono ^4.12.34, @hono/node-server ^1.19.15, body-parser ^2.3.0, @babel/core ^7.29.6, protobufjs ^7.6.3, @ai-sdk/provider-utils@3.0.25 -> ^3.0.28 (scoped); qs 6.15.2 -> 6.16.0, ip-address ^9.0.5 -> ^10.3.1, brace-expansion ^2.0.1 -> ^2.1.4.
- **Deliberate non-fixes (documented, verified live)**: (1) path-to-regexp NOT overridden - Express 5's router needs the 8.x API while tsoa's bundled express@4 needs 0.1.x; a flat override forces one API onto both and breaks all route registration (caught by aiStudioSync.test.ts). (2) ts-deepmerge stays at 7.x - the 8.0.0 fix is a breaking major for tsoa's alpha CLI (dev-only path, prototype-pollution DoS class, not reachable in prod runtime).
- **Remaining 2 alerts**: path-to-regexp <0.1.13 via tsoa>express@4 (high; real fix is tsoa 7 GA or dropping tsoa - both queued, see CC-2026-09-24-001 context) and ts-deepmerge <8 (moderate, dev-only). Both are accepted-risk with the reasoning above, not oversights.
- **Incident note**: three concurrent pnpm install processes were left running by earlier tool timeouts and deadlocked on store locks (30+ min of thrash). Killed via taskkill; single-process install then completed normally. Lesson recorded: on this OneDrive-mounted workspace, never overlap pnpm operations.
- **Verification**: pnpm audit 35 -> 2 (1 high 1 moderate, both accepted-risk); npx tsc --noEmit clean; full vitest suite 437/437 (45 files).
- **Rollback**: git revert the pnpm-workspace.yaml overrides block + pnpm-lock.yaml, then pnpm install.
## CC-2026-09-25-007 - Ops Agent / DAG reliability + chat UX / Fake agent ids killed, cards editable after runs
- **Date**: 2026-09-25
- **Type**: bugfix (DAG execution) + UX improvement (Ops Agent chat)
- **Scope**: server/controllers/orchestrator-execute.ts; server/domain/schemas.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator.ts; server/execution/queue-processor.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator-execute.test.ts; client/src/components/OpsAgentChat.tsx
- **Trigger**: Robert's DAG proposals failing with "Execution Blocker Detected" and an audit_logs insert bind error; the Ops Agent's own chat diagnosis blamed a NOT NULL agent_id and proposed a schema migration.
- **Diagnosis correction (honesty)**: the chat diagnosis was wrong on both counts - audit_logs.agent_id is ALREADY nullable (schema + drizzle snapshot 0000), and no code path writes '' to it (traced all ~15 insert sites; only queue-processor touches step.agentId, which binds SQL NULL for agent-less steps). The real bug: the DAG generators INVENT agent ids that are neither UUIDs nor real rows ("agent-sal-crm", "agent-{dept}-specialist" in the fallback generator; "agent_ops_lead" hardcoded into the Add Custom Step button). Those strings were synced into workflow_steps.agent_id - a uuid column - and the run died on uuid binding. The Ops Agent pattern-matched the surface error instead of tracing writers; noted as a known limitation of self-diagnosis.
- **Fix (execution path)**: orchestrator-execute now resolves proposal agentIds against the agents table before sync - only ids that exist as real rows pass through, everything else binds NULL. workflowStepSchema.agentId is now uuid-validated so LLM-invented ids are rejected at generation time. The fallback generator no longer emits agent ids at all. The two audit insert sites defensively normalize `step.agentId || null`.
- **Fix (chat UX)**: proposal cards stay interactive after a run - failed/completed runs no longer lock editing; a failed banner shows the run id; "Re-run Amended DAG" re-executes after edits; Revise opens an inline textarea (replacing the browser prompt()) that sends the feedback as a normal chat turn; a Clear Chat button (trash icon) resets the conversation to start a new task without old context.
- **Tests**: 2 new tests (fake-id-to-NULL resolution across real/invented/absent agentIds; source-level regression against reintroducing the fake ids). Full suite 439/439, tsc clean.
- **Rollback**: revert the six listed files.
## CC-2026-09-25-008 - Audit-log insert cannot fail a step / dangling agent_id self-heal
- **Date**: 2026-09-26
- **Type**: bugfix (DAG execution resilience)
- **Scope**: server/execution/audit-logger.ts (new); server/execution/audit-logger.test.ts (new); server/execution/queue-processor.ts; server/db.ts
- **Trigger**: re-running the existing Initiate Partnership Workflow failed step 2 (Market Analyst agent) with an audit_logs insert error - even though the agent itself SUCCEEDED (1995 tokens, searchLocalFiles executed non-simulated).
- **Root cause**: the success-path audit insert in queue-processor sat unprotected inside the step try/catch, so its throw bubbled into the failure handler and marked the step failed after the work was done. The insert itself died on the agent_id FK: Postgres FKs are table-global, and the first two FK columns (workspace_id, workflow_id) provably existed (the run and its steps were just read from those tables), so the violation must be agent_id 75ee7a40-... having no agents row. The step was synced BEFORE CC-2026-09-25-007; onDelete set-null only fires on later deletes and cannot heal an id that was never valid at insert time.
- **Fix (execution path)**: new insertAuditLog helper with a non-fatal contract - never throws; on SQLSTATE 23503 naming agent_id it retries once with agentId NULL and records agentIdDropped + droppedAgentId in policy_checks (audit trail stays honest about the drop); every other failure degrades to a warning. Both queue-processor insert sites (success + failure path) now go through it. Telemetry failure can no longer kill the work it evidences.
- **Fix (data path)**: ensureDatabaseSchema gained a boot-time scrub that nulls workflow_steps.agent_id values with no matching agents row (same dangling-id class; cleans all old workflows in one pass).
- **Tests**: 6 new tests in audit-logger.test.ts (clean insert; FK fallback to NULL with policy_checks evidence; drizzle-wrapped cause-chain detection; no retry when agentId already NULL; unrelated failure degrades without throwing; retry failure returns false without throwing). Full suite 445/445, tsc clean.
- **Rollback**: revert the four listed files (delete the two new ones).
## CC-2026-09-25-009 - audit_logs schema/DDL drift found and fixed / the real root cause of the 9/26 step failure
- **Date**: 2026-09-27
- **Type**: bugfix (schema drift) + diagnosis correction
- **Scope**: server/db.ts; server/execution/audit-logger.ts; drizzle/0012-audit-logs-cancel-columns.sql (new); drizzle/meta/_journal.json
- **Trigger**: Robert re-ran the Initiate Partnership Workflow; step 2 failed on the audit_logs insert. CC-2026-09-25-008 had made the insert non-fatal but left the root cause unexplained.
- **Diagnosis correction (honesty)**: the 008 agent_id FK theory was WRONG. Live-DB probes (read-only, Infisical-wrapped) proved agent 75ee7a40-... exists (Operations Analyst, created 2026-08-24) and zero workflow_steps rows have dangling agent_ids. The real cause was found by replicating the exact INSERT inside BEGIN/ROLLBACK: SQLSTATE 42703 - column "cancel_requested" of relation "audit_logs" does not exist.
- **Root cause**: commit f33439d8 (2026-09-23) added cancelRequested/cancelledAt to schema.ts on BOTH workflow_runs and audit_logs, but 0010-runner-hardening.sql and the ensureDatabaseSchema self-heal only mirrored the workflow_runs pair. The live drizzle journal stops at 0002 (dev relies on the self-heal), so nothing ever shipped the audit_logs DDL. Every audit insert naming the new columns failed from 2026-09-23 22:35 (last successful audit row) until today - silently at the ~15 guarded insert sites, fatally at the queue-processor success path until 008 unwrapped it. Drizzle builds INSERT column lists from schema.ts, so no code change could have avoided it.
- **Fix**: (1) ensureDatabaseSchema now adds the audit_logs cancel_requested/cancelled_at pair (idempotent); the running tsx-watch dev server applied it on the db.ts edit, verified live. (2) Migration 0012 mirrors the same DDL for journal/prod completeness. (3) insertAuditLog detects 42703 in the cause chain and logs a loud, actionable SCHEMA/DDL DRIFT error naming the self-heal and migration file, so the next drift is found in minutes instead of four days.
- **Live verification**: the exact previously-failing insert (same 20-column list, same param values) now succeeds under rollback; all schema.ts-declared audit_logs columns exist; the live dev DB shows no missing/dropped columns remaining.
- **Known landmine (pre-existing, noted)**: drizzle meta snapshots stop at 0004 while hand-written SQL migrations continue to 0012; a future drizzle-kit generate will diff from 0004 and emit a noisy superset migration. Schedule a snapshot-baseline reconciliation before the next drizzle-kit generate.
- **Tests**: full suite 445/445, tsc clean. The 008 FK-fallback and boot scrub are retained as defense-in-depth (FK violations remain possible for future deletions).
- **Rollback**: revert the four listed files (delete 0012).
## CC-2026-09-25-010 - drizzle-kit generate reconciled / code-truth baseline replaces relic snapshots
- **Date**: 2026-09-27
- **Type**: build-tooling fix (migration metadata baseline)
- **Scope**: drizzle/ (restructure); drizzle/meta/_journal.json + 0000_snapshot.json; server/db.ts; server/execution/audit-logger.ts; drizzle/legacy/* (12 superseded SQL migrations + 5 relic snapshots moved, history preserved)
- **Trigger**: the known landmine from 009: meta snapshots stopped at 0004 while hand-written SQL continued to 0012, so the next drizzle-kit generate would diff against a four-week-old schema and emit a destructive superset migration.
- **Method**: two disposable introspection/generate runs into temp dirs. DB-truth snapshot via drizzle-kit pull (with a sed-cloned temp config, since --config cannot combine with --out); code-truth snapshot via generate against schema.ts into an empty out. Column-set diff of the two proved ZERO columns declared in code are missing from the live DB (42703 class fully eradicated) and zero stray live-only columns.
- **Decision (recorded honestly)**: chose CODE-TRUTH baseline (snapshot of schema.ts) over DB-truth. The DB-truth path produced a residual 0001 that was 90% cosmetic churn (drizzle naming convention vs Postgres default FK/index names) plus four real attribute diffs; adopting it would have baked permanent churn into every future generate. Code-truth makes generate minimal by construction and routes real convergence through the proven ensureDatabaseSchema self-heal.
- **Live convergence applied (self-heal, idempotent)**: workspaces.trial_ends_at default now()+30d; agents.uptime DROP DEFAULT + DROP NOT NULL (the honesty-audit 99.9% default is now gone from the DATABASE too, not just code); workflow_artifacts.verification_notes real rubric default; workflow_share_tokens.last_accessed_at backfilled from created_at then SET DEFAULT now() + SET NOT NULL. All four verified live by pg_catalog probe after the tsx-watch restart applied them.
- **Accepted (documented, not chased)**: FK and index NAME differences between drizzle convention and Postgres defaults remain on the live DB. They are functionally identical; chasing them would trade real risk for cosmetic parity. The residual diff is deterministic and appears in every future generate only if schema.ts changes shape.
- **Validation**: drizzle-kit generate now reports No schema changes, nothing to migrate against the fresh baseline; full suite 445/445; tsc clean; change-control green. db:push remains untouched - dev continues on the self-heal path; the journal baseline keeps future migrate runs coherent for prod.
- **Rollback**: git revert restores the previous drizzle/ layout and self-heal block.
## CC-2026-09-25-011 - Ops Agent UX: editable step types, honest estimates, question-aware routing, persistent threads
- **Date**: 2026-09-27
- **Type**: feature (Ops Agent chat) + honesty fix
- **Scope**: client/src/components/OpsAgentChat.tsx; server/controllers/orchestrator.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator-execute.ts; server/controllers/ops-chat.ts (new); server/controllers/ops-agent-routing.test.ts (new); server/domain/schemas.ts; server/schema.ts; server/db.ts; server/routes/api.ts; drizzle/0001_steep_marten_broadcloak.sql (generated)
- **Trigger**: Robert asked how the Ops Agent runs now and whether the four known UX gaps could be closed; he approved all four.
- **Fix 1 (editable step types)**: proposal-card steps now carry a type selector (agent/guardrail/action/destination) so a human-approval gate can be inserted without a chat round-trip. Custom steps now default to agent (the honest general case) instead of ACTION. classifyStepType gained the action type (human-gated outbound via the actions router) and is exported for tests.
- **Fix 2 (honest estimates)**: estimatedCostPerRun/estimatedLatencySeconds are optional-nullable in the proposal schema with explicit never-guess descriptions; the deterministic fallback no longer emits the fabricated 0.02/15s numbers; the card shows the model estimate only when supplied, and after a run shows the REAL measured cost and latency from the run rows. Custom cost field added to the client run result.
- **Fix 3 (question-aware routing)**: mode routing extracted into exported shouldProposeWorkflow with a strong-imperative opener rule and an interrogative guard - "run that by me again" and "what runs do we have?" now get conversational answers, while "build the onboarding DAG, ok?" still proposes. 9 regression tests pin the matrix.
- **Fix 4 (persistent threads)**: new ops_agent_messages table (workspace-scoped, client-generated thread id, denormalized proposal/runResult/executionStatus) + REST endpoints (GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE under /api/orchestrator/chat) in the new ops-chat controller. The client hydrates the thread on mount, persists user/assistant messages (best-effort, never blocks chat), PATCHes run outcomes onto the stored assistant message, and Clear Chat deletes the server thread and issues a fresh id. Watchdog reports stay ephemeral by design.
- **Validation**: full suite 454/454 (9 new), tsc clean, change-control green. drizzle-kit generate emitted a clean single-table migration (0001) and a second run reports no changes - first live exercise of the 010 code-truth baseline, no legacy churn. Table DDL also added to ensureDatabaseSchema so dev self-heals at next boot.
- **Rollback**: revert the listed files; drop ops_agent_messages if desired.
