## CC-2026-09-25-014 - Agent Execution / Action drafts / Markdown-fenced drafts no longer killed the HubSpot dispatch step
- **Date**: 2026-09-27
- **Type**: bugfix (human-gated action pipeline, CC-2026-09-23-018 surface)
- **Scope**: server/execution/action-drafter.ts; server/execution/action-drafter.test.ts
- **Trigger**: the Founder RoundTable Event Data Ingest DAG failed its action step with "Action draft invalid: unknown or missing connector (registered: hubspot_contact_upsert)" even though the raw draft visibly contained the correct connector.
- **Root cause**: three-link chain. (1) When model output is not clean JSON, agent-runner wraps it as `{ result: text }`; the model emitted the draft inside a ```json fence, so the parse failed and the whole fenced string landed in result. (2) draftActionPayload's result branch only passed the string through if it STARTED with `{`; a code fence does not, so it fell through to JSON.stringify of the entire wrapper. (3) parseActionDraft then saw the wrapper object - no connector key at top level - and failed, while extractJsonObject (which already handles fenced and mixed text) never got the chance. The drafter's own system suffix even demanded raw JSON, but the model fenced anyway; the fallback should have been tolerant the same way the parser already is.
- **Fix**: draftActionPayload now passes raw `result` strings through untouched, letting extractJsonObject pull the embedded draft out (it already strips fences and prose). Nothing about the strict contract changed: unknown connectors, missing titles, and non-object payloads still fail the step honestly; a result string with no JSON draft in it still fails validation. Also tightened the drafting prompt to say no markdown code fences explicitly. No changes to connectors, queue-processor, the actions router, or HubSpot itself - the dispatch ledger, approval gates, and SAIF tripwires are untouched.
- **Tests**: 4 new regression tests pin the production shape (fenced JSON inside result unwraps to a valid hubspot_contact_upsert draft; prose-wrapped JSON still parses; a result string with no draft fails honestly) plus the existing strict-contract tests unchanged; drafter suite 12/12, tsc --noEmit clean, change-control green.
- **Robert action**: re-run the failed Founder RoundTable run - the HubSpot step will now draft successfully and pause for approval as designed. Confirm the vault HUBSPOT_PAT is healthy in Infisical before approving the dispatch.
- **Rollback**: revert the two listed files (one-code-branch change).
- **Ops-agent cross-check (same day, follow-up)**: Robert relayed the ops agent's endorsement, which bundled three claims that did not survive contact with the live rows: (1) it attributed a "capability check failure in Step 1" to run e977032c of the Founder RoundTable DAG - the run exists but belongs to the unrelated Daily Agency Operations & State Report workflow (c8b910e7) and is dated 2026-09-05; that workflow's last three runs COMPLETED, and its historical failures are a real refusal-phrase artifact ("my capabilities are limited to..." tripping detectAgentRefusal), not a configuration bug - the deterministic fallback voice says "limited to" and pre-dated the quota fix. (2) The genuinely failed run is c5a8f2d5 of the NEW Founder RoundTable Post-Event Engagement & Nurture workflow (c93d4c1c, itself proposed by the ops agent earlier today): steps 0-4 completed; step 5 (Update HubSpot CRM with Event Engagement) failed at 16:17 local with the exact fenced-result parse error 014 fixes - the fix landed ~16:28, so the failure predates it; expected to pass on re-run. (3) Its proposed "HubSpot Connector Fix Validation" DAG was REJECTED without execution: step 4 would have dispatched a synthetic test@example.com contact to the LIVE production HubSpot CRM (connector contract validates the payload cleanly), polluting the real CRM with test data; its guardrail step reviews formatting but can catch nothing (the dispatch ledger validates before parking); and its step 3-to-4 chaining misreads the actual pipeline (guardrails pause the run; action steps draft and pause again; approval happens in the actions router, not in a later step). Validation instead happens via re-running the real DAG with real event data. Read-only DB probe followed the CC-2026-09-25-009 pattern (SELECT only, Infisical-wrapped, no secret values printed; probe script deleted after use).
- **Validation attempt result (same day)**: end-to-end validation is currently BLOCKED on the LLM credential, not on the 014 fix. Run d68deaa9 was triggered against workflow c93d4c1c with the REAL URC-Phase1-CRM-Lite tracker rows Robert provided (10 contacts, Event IDs FR-001..FR003/BC-001, verbatim from the sheet; no attendee fields invented - only Sandra Hill has a recorded email, and the ingest context explicitly forbids the agents from fabricating addresses/chat/Q&A data). Executed through the REAL processPendingRuns pipeline (the local dev server was found DOWN - an unrelated ~6MB node process squats port 3000 and 404s everything; the app is not listening on 3001-3005 - so the queue processor was invoked directly via a kept script, scripts/execute-pending-runs.ts). The run failed honestly at step 1 before reaching the HubSpot action step: all three fallback models returned 401. Root cause identified by a no-exposure probe (scripts/diagnose-gemini-env.mjs, prints statuses/prefixes and Google's error only): the GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY value stored in Infisical is a 53-char Google OAuth ACCESS TOKEN (prefix AQ.A...), not an AI Studio API key (39-char, AIza...). Google rejects it with ACCESS_TOKEN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED. It was evidently still valid at 16:15 today (the earlier run made real 22.7s/5.4s model calls) and expired within the hour - OAuth access tokens are short-lived by design. CC-2026-09-25-004 already established the correct fix path. ROBERT ACTIONS: (1) aistudio.google.com > Get API key > create key (starts AIza); (2) replace the GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY value in Infisical dev (the AQ.A... token is wrong AND dead); (3) restart the dev server (pnpm dev) and retire whatever holds port 3000; (4) re-run validation: pnpm exec infisical run --env=dev -- node scripts/trigger-roundtable-validation.mjs then node scripts/execute-pending-runs.ts (or just trigger from the UI once the server is up). Kept scripts (documented tools, not scratch): trigger-roundtable-validation.mjs (re-trigger with sheet data), execute-pending-runs.ts (real-pipeline executor), diagnose-gemini-env.mjs (credential probe, no secret material printed). The run row d68deaa9 stands as the honest failure record.
- **Diagnosis correction (same day, after Robert reported AI Studio only issues AQ.-prefixed keys)**: the earlier read of the Infisical value as an "expired OAuth access token" was WRONG. The AQ.-prefixed value is Google's NEW AI Studio key format: since June 2026 AI Studio issues "Authentication Keys" (AQ....) platform-wide, retiring the legacy AIza "traffic keys" - not an org-policy artifact and not short-lived by design. Dual-mode probe results with the stored key: x-goog-api-key header -> 401 ACCESS_TOKEN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED; Authorization Bearer -> 401 API_KEY_SERVICE_BLOCKED (Google classifies the credential as API-key-type but blocks it from generativelanguage.googleapis.com). That means the remaining unblock question is whether API-key access to the Generative Language API can be enabled for this key/project (enable the API, or the org's API-key restrictions cover it - consistent with the org constraints documented in CC-2026-09-24-006) or whether this org cannot use Gemini API keys at all. Fallback path that needs NO Google credential: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is present and verified live (CC-2026-09-24-005) and @ai-sdk/anthropic is already a dependency; adding an Anthropic leg to the agent-runner's provider fallback chain is a code option if the Google routes stay blocked.
- **Robert decision executed + second diagnosis correction (same day)**: Robert chose "enable the Gemini API". gcloud (authenticated as agentlab.tech@gmail.com) confirmed generativelanguage.googleapis.com was NOT enabled on any candidate project while aiplatform.googleapis.com WAS enabled on the org project; enabled the Gemini API on the org project (project-36330a6c-5e91-4901-9dd) and, after the stored key STILL returned API_KEY_SERVICE_BLOCKED, on the remaining visible projects (cs-poc-*, portable-founder-dashboard, project-...-abf80; the three gen-lang-client auto-projects already had it). Key still service-blocked after propagation - so the AQ.-key rejection persists regardless of project enablement, consistent with the community reports of AQ-key incompatibility cohorts; this is now a Google-side limitation we cannot fix from the account. CORRECTION to CC-2026-09-24-005's framing carried forward: the Anthropic key "verified live" check only exercised the free models-list endpoint - it never proved paid inference. Live proof via the new runner leg: the stored ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is AUTH-VALID but the account has ZERO CREDITS ("Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API").
- **CC-2026-09-25-015 - Agent Execution / Provider resilience / Anthropic fallback leg in the agent runner**: with the Gemini credential unusable from our side, the runner gained a cross-provider last resort: the legacy 3-attempt Gemini chain is unchanged, then - ONLY when ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is configured - one final attempt runs claude-haiku-4-5 via @ai-sdk/anthropic (already a dependency) through the same generateText call with the full tool contract. Without the key, behavior is byte-identical to before. Honesty details: cost ternary prices the Anthropic branch at Claude rates (1.0 in / 5.0 out per 1M) instead of silently charging Claude calls at Gemini rates; the result carries modelUsed ('anthropic:<id>') so telemetry can show which provider actually answered; the retries-exhausted error now names the REAL last attempted model (the old index arithmetic misattributed it to a Gemini model). 3 source-pin regression tests in server/execution/anthropic-fallback.test.ts; tsc clean; execution suite 85/85.
- **Live validation status (end of day)**: run d68deaa9 executed twice through the real pipeline, failed honestly both times at step 1 BEFORE the HubSpot action step, now with a fully characterized error chain: attempts 1-3 Gemini 401 (AQ. key rejected by Google service-side), attempt 4 Claude 402 (no credits). Validation is one Robert action away: fund the Anthropic account (console.anthropic.com > Plans & Billing > credits) - after which the same run should proceed to the HubSpot step and park as awaiting_approval, completing the 014 verification. Kept scripts: reset-run.mjs added (reset a failed run to pending); trigger/execute/diagnose scripts unchanged.
- **Gemini-recommended dual-header workaround TESTED AND REJECTED (same day)**: a snippet (attributed to Gemini) suggested forcing Authorization: Bearer alongside the SDK's x-goog-api-key for AQ. keys. Probed directly with the stored key: dual header on generativelanguage v1beta -> 401 with a NEW message - "API keys are not supported by this API. Expected OAuth2 access token or other authentication credentials that assert a principal" (reason API_KEY_SERVICE_BLOCKED); same on v1alpha; Bearer-only unchanged. VERDICT: this account's gateway treats the Gemini API as PRINCIPAL-ONLY - no API key format (AIza or AQ.) can authenticate; Gemini's snippet does not fix this account. Vertex express-mode probes (global + project-qualified URLs, x-goog-api-key / Bearer / query-param key) all returned bare 404s identical to a no-auth control - inconclusive, no auth signal. TWO VIABLE ROUTES REMAIN: (a) fund Anthropic credits - one click, the runner leg is already built and proven to reach the API; (b) keyless OAuth user principal via gcloud ADC (`gcloud auth application-default login` once, interactive) plus a small google-ai.ts extension to mint access tokens from ADC user credentials - the "credentials that assert a principal" the API says it wants, org-policy-compliant (no service-account key, no API key), and gcloud itself already proves user-principal tokens are accepted on this account (all the service-enable calls ran under it).
## CC-2026-09-25-016 - Google AI auth / ADC user-principal path for the principal-only gateway
- **Date**: 2026-09-27
- **Type**: feature (auth) + unblock route (Robert chose the ADC option)
- **Scope**: server/_core/google-ai.ts; server/_core/google-ai.test.ts; scripts/probe-adc-scope.mjs (new, kept)
- **Probe evidence chain** (all statuses only, no secret material printed): existing ADC file EXISTS and mints tokens; plain user-principal Bearer against generativelanguage = 403 ACCESS_TOKEN_SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT (auth OK, scope missing - the first non-401 of the day); minting a refresh-grant token WITH the generative-language scope fails invalid_scope (the original ADC consent did not include it); minting with cloud-platform succeeds but the API still demands its own scope; gcloud CLI token equally scope-limited. CONCLUSION: the ADC path is correct but the existing credential was consented without the Gemini scope; one interactive re-login adds it.
- **Robert action (one command, browser consent)**: gcloud auth application-default login --scopes=https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform,https://www.googleapis.com/auth/generative-language — then verify instantly with: node scripts/probe-adc-scope.mjs (should print SUCCESS). No restart needed at runtime: the factory resolves ADC per call and caches tokens for the grant lifetime; clearGoogleTokenCache is available if needed.
- **Implementation**: resolveAdcUserCredentials (explicit GOOGLE_AI_ADC_FILE / GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS short-circuit - an explicit pointer that is absent means NO ADC, never machine-default fallback; otherwise the standard gcloud ADC location) + mintAdcUserToken (refresh grant, Node fetch, no new deps). Precedence everywhere: service account (org policy) -> ADC user principal -> API-key fallback; the ADC path deliberately outranks API keys in createGoogleProvider because keys are PROVEN unusable on this gateway (a stale key must not shadow the working principal). isGoogleAiConfigured counts ADC unless GOOGLE_AI_ADC_DISABLED=1. Tokens cached with the same 5-min-early refresh as the SA path. The no-credential error now states the principal-only reality and prints the exact gcloud command.
- **Tests**: google-ai.test.ts extended (ADC-configured true, hermetic no-credential case via explicit-absent override, GOOGLE_AI_ADC_DISABLED semantics); 13/13 in the file, _core 19/19, tsc clean. A test-caught real semantic bug: the first resolver draft fell back to machine ADC when an explicit override was absent - fixed to credential-isolation semantics before landing.
- **Rollback**: revert google-ai.ts + test file; delete scripts/probe-adc-scope.mjs.

## CC-2026-09-30-008 - Security / Dependencies / Main app audit driven to zero; firebase + tsoa removed as vestigial
- **Date**: 2026-09-30
- **Type**: dependency remediation (no runtime code changed)
- **Scope**: package.json; pnpm-workspace.yaml; pnpm-lock.yaml; agentlabhs/src/app/{cards,pages,settings}/package-lock.json; pr-posse/package-lock.json
- **Trigger**: GitHub reported 260 vulnerabilities (14 critical) on the default branch; Robert asked what can be done.
- **Diagnosis**: the count was cross-manifest aggregation; the main app had 4 findings (2 high) and both highs traced to vestigial dependencies — firebase (declared prod dep, zero imports, no config files) and tsoa (no config, no scripts, no imports). tsoa was the ONLY reason the un-overridable express@4/path-to-regexp 0.1.x tree existed (the exact blocker documented in CC-2026-09-25-006).
- **Change**: removed firebase + tsoa; the @grpc/grpc-js and ts-deepmerge findings died with them; cleaned workspace build-allow lists; lockfile-only audit fixes in the three agentlabhs sub-projects (all 0 now); pr-posse reduced to 2 findings requiring a breaking agentic-flow major — filed as a dated decision in scheduled-change-queue rather than forced.
- **Verification**: pnpm audit clean ("No known vulnerabilities found"); vitest 487/487; tsc noise unchanged (pre-existing cookie@2); change-control green.
- **Rollback**: `pnpm add firebase@^12.18.0 && pnpm add -D tsoa@7.0.0-alpha.0 && pnpm install`, or revert the three files.

## CC-2026-09-30-009 - Security / Dependencies / CORRECTION: firebase restored at root; grpc high killed via override instead
- **Date**: 2026-09-30
- **Type**: correction to CC-2026-09-30-008 (dependency restore + override)
- **Scope**: package.json; pnpm-workspace.yaml; pnpm-lock.yaml
- **Trigger**: Robert corrected the 008 diagnosis: "Firebase is used in a couple of the built in and standalone apps."
- **Evidence**: `agentlab/package.json` (standalone app, root workspace has NO `packages:` field so it installs independently) declares `firebase ^12.18.0`; with no node_modules of its own on this machine, its `require('firebase')` resolves UPWARD into root `node_modules` — the exact mechanism 008's import scan (server/ + client/src/ only) could not see. Post-restore proof: node resolution from `agentlab/` finds `<root>/node_modules/firebase`.
- **Change**: firebase@^12.18.0 restored at root; '@firebase/util' restored to onlyBuiltDependencies/allowBuilds; NEW override '@grpc/grpc-js': ^1.14.5 kills BOTH grpc advisories at the patched line so the audit stays clean WITH firebase present (resolution verified 1.14.5 across the tree; pnpm audit still reports zero findings; suite 487/487). tsoa removal NOT reverted — it remains config-less, script-less, and import-less.
- **Lesson recorded**: an import scan of the entrypoints is NOT proof a dependency is unused in a repo containing standalone apps that resolve upward; Robert's operational knowledge outranks a negative grep. Future dep removals need a consumer survey across ALL manifests + an upward-resolution check first.
- **Rollback**: remove the firebase dep and the @grpc/grpc-js override; revert the three files.

## CC-2026-09-30-010 - Dependencies / Standalone-app own-install attempt: infeasible in agent session; queued with runbook; partial install safely removed
- **Date**: 2026-09-30
- **Type**: operational attempt + queue entry (no repo dependency changes)
- **Scope**: agentlab/node_modules (created, then removed); docs/operations/scheduled-change-queue.md (CC-2026-09-30-009 queue row); this register
- **Trigger**: Robert approved giving the standalone apps their own installs (per CC-2026-09-30-009's upward-resolution finding).
- **Discovery**: `agentlab/` is GITIGNORED (.gitignore line `agentlab/`) — invisible to git, Dependabot, and repo tooling; explains both the phantom-dependency blindness and why GitHub's alert count will never include it. Has NO lockfile and NO node_modules of its own on this machine.
- **Attempt and constraint**: three `pnpm install --ignore-workspace` runs in `agentlab/` were each truncated at the 10-minute tool ceiling (~880-package install on OneDrive); a second concurrent installer spawned by a retry reproduced the EXACT OneDrive pnpm-deadlock pattern documented 2026-09-25 (both killed via taskkill per the register's lesson); a detached relaunch died immediately because agent tool shells kill child processes between calls. Conclusion: the first full install must be run from a REAL terminal, not an agent session.
- **State restored**: the partial install (879 staged packages, no lockfile, firebase not yet linked) was REMOVED — a partial node_modules is worse than none because it shadows the root tree the apps borrow from. `agentlab/` is back to the exact pre-session state (no node_modules; upward resolution into root, where firebase@^12.18.0 + the @grpc/grpc-js >=1.14.5 override now live).
- **Queued**: CC-2026-09-30-009 in scheduled-change-queue carries the exact commands, the deadlock rule, the real-terminal requirement, and post-install verification steps (resolution check inside the app dir + the app's own audit).
- **AMENDED 2026-09-30 (CC-2026-09-30-011)**: Robert completed the install from a real terminal the same evening — agentlab/ is now self-contained (own node_modules + lockfile) and verified. See CC-2026-09-30-011; queue row CC-2026-09-30-009 marked Done.

## CC-2026-09-30-012 - Fullstack / Ops Agent / Google retired gemini-2.5-flash: every chat answered with a canned DAG; shared model chain + honest fallback shipped
- **Date**: 2026-09-30
- **Type**: bugfix (model availability) + honesty fix (fallback fabrication)
- **Scope**: server/_core/google-ai.ts; server/_core/google-ai.test.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator.ts; server/controllers/orchestrator-fallback.test.ts (new); server/founder-intake/router.ts; server/founder-intake/gemini-intake.test.ts; server/controllers/{assessment-questions,icp,artifacts,agents,snapshots,campaigns,fulfillment,aiStudioSync,marketplace}.ts; server/execution/billing-engine.ts; server/_core/llm.ts; server/routes/api.ts; client/src/pages/{OpsCleanupAgent,Agents,Settings}.tsx; scripts/probe-gemini-chain.ts (new, kept); this register
- **Trigger**: Robert's report + screenshots — the Ops Agent answers every prompt with the same "Vision purpose" DAG proposal; two browser restarts did not change it.
- **Root cause**: production log evidence: `404 NOT_FOUND — This model models/gemini-2.5-flash is no longer available to new users. Please update your code to use models/gemini-3.8-flash`. The chat handler hardcoded the retired id on both the proposal and conversational paths; its catch-all called generateFallbackWorkflowProposal for EVERY failure, so questions died into the canned 4-step template that spoke first-person ("I have analyzed your request..."). "Vision purpose" was the template matching a real workflow name in the workspace.
- **Change**: (1) shared GOOGLE_MODEL_CHAIN + withGoogleModelChain in google-ai.ts — the alias chain agent-runner has used since 09-28, now repo-wide; (2) orchestrator chat: both paths iterate the chain, modelUsed reports the id that actually answered, and the catch degrades mode-aware and honest — no proposal object, no fabricated prose, error logged, "Tried: <chain>" shown; (3) founder-intake callGemini chain healed (its 2.5→2.0→1.5 chain was 100% dead — the public intake chat was silently scripted-fallback for new accounts); (4) assessment-questions, icp, artifacts, billing-engine, /api/debug/llm onto the chain; (5) honesty labels: audit-log rows on deterministic paths relabeled "not-llm-dispatch"; agents.ts seeds/defaults and llm.ts Forge leg updated; (6) client model pickers (Ops Agent, Agents, Settings) offer current ids; Settings "Ultra-Fast 14ms" fiction dropped from the label.
- **Environment repair (same change)**: root node_modules was missing the @trpc/react-query junction (declared dep; plain `pnpm install` reported "Already up to date" without healing it) — restored with a targeted add; ~11 phantom client typecheck errors disappeared; manifests unchanged.
- **Verification**: vitest 495/495 (8 new: chain order + retired-id exclusion, honest fallback in conversational AND proposal modes, modelUsed on success, source pins); tsc at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline; scripts/probe-gemini-chain.ts live: gemini-flash-latest / gemini-3.8-flash / gemini-pro-latest all return "pong" (131/66/85 tokens).
- **Deploy note**: DEPLOYED same night via the manual path (push-to-deploy still broken, missing GCP_SA_KEY): build 88c52ba4 SUCCESS 6m58s from 0125f2c6, new revision Ready (deployed 2026-10-01T02:53:34Z). Post-deploy verification: /api/health 200; /api/debug/llm makes a REAL chain call → success:true, "ok via gemini-flash-latest"; /api/dashboard/llm-ping alive:true 838ms. Robert retests: hard refresh /ops-agent, ask the same question — expect a real conversational answer with "Model: gemini-flash-latest | token usage reported".
- **Robert CONFIRMED the fix (22:16 local screenshot)**: the same question now returns a real conversational answer — "Model: gemini-flash-latest | 4384 tokens", run id/date quoted from real telemetry (e977032c, Sep 5, Daily Agency Operations & State Report). Two residual honesty findings from that answer, hardening shipped same night: (1) the model CONFABULATED an "AgentLab DAG Orchestration Engine v2.4" (no such version exists) — conversational system prompt now forbids any version/engine claim not verbatim in the telemetry block; (2) raw **markdown** rendered literally in the directive card — conversational prompt now requires plain text only. Source-pin test added; targeted 15/15; change-control green.
- **Follow-ups recorded (not forced tonight)**: workspaces.defaultModel is stored via Settings but consumed by NO call site (display-only — queue a wiring decision); schema.ts DB column defaults still say gemini-1.5-pro (dormant — columns are always explicitly set; changing them requires coordinated self-heal DDL); docs/marketing copy still says "Gemini 2.5 Flash" (descriptive only).
- **Rollback**: revert the listed files (single commit); the chain helper is additive so a revert restores the previous behavior including its 404s.
- **Rollback**: none needed (net state change is the two docs rows; node_modules removal restored the prior state).

## CC-2026-10-01-002 - Fullstack / Ops Agent / why chat persistence is empty in production (root cause) + decision to wire the /ops-agent page before the next partner demo
- **Date**: 2026-10-01
- **Type**: investigation (root cause) + decision record (no code changes yet; implementation queued)
- **Scope**: this register; docs/operations/scheduled-change-queue.md; MEMORY.md; scripts/probe-ops-agent-session.mjs (evidence tool used)
- **Trigger**: CC-2026-10-01-001's evidence probe found `ops_agent_messages` at 0 rows in production despite persistence shipping in CC-2026-09-25-011 (2026-09-27). Robert asked for the root cause and a persist-or-not decision before the next partner demo.
- **Root cause (code path evidence)**: chat persistence is CLIENT-DRIVEN, not server-side. The four endpoints (GET/POST `/api/orchestrator/chat/:threadId`, POST `/:threadId/messages`, PATCH `/chat/messages/:id`, DELETE `/:threadId` — server/routes/api.ts:173-176 → server/controllers/ops-chat.ts) are passive: they only write when a client calls them. Exactly one client surface calls them: the global floating widget `client/src/components/OpsAgentChat.tsx` (thread id `opsagent_<uuid>` in localStorage, hydrate on mount, `persistMessage` POST per message, PATCH run outcome; watchdog reports deliberately not persisted). The `/ops-agent` PAGE — `client/src/pages/OpsCleanupAgent.tsx`, the surface used for the Dheerendar demo — POSTs directly to `/api/orchestrator/chat` for proposals and the execute endpoint for runs, and contains NO reference to the thread endpoints, threadId, or localStorage (verified by grep over the whole file). `CommandCenter.tsx`'s chat shares the same non-persisting shape. Therefore: page-based sessions (every partner demo) produce zero chat rows; only widget sessions persist. Nothing is broken — the capability simply was never wired into the page.
- **Decision (Robert delegated the call): persist — YES, before the next partner-facing demo.** Wiring `OpsCleanupAgent.tsx` to the existing endpoints mirrors the widget's already-shipped pattern (thread id in localStorage, hydrate on mount, persist user/assistant messages including proposal cards and answers to clarifying questions, PATCH `runResult`/`executionStatus` when the run completes). Client-only; no server changes; lands in production via the manual Cloud Build path. Queued as queue row CC-2026-10-01-002 (Fast Lane-eligible: partner demos are release-adjacent).
- **Honest caveat until it ships**: demos through `/ops-agent` remain half-evidenced — the run half is provable from `workflow_runs` (CC-2026-10-01-001), the conversation half is not. Do not describe any demo transcript as "recorded" until the wiring ships and a live write is verified.
- **Rollback**: not applicable (records only; the queued implementation is additive and revertable per its own review).

## CC-2026-10-01-004 - Integrations / Upwork MCP / endpoint live + OS registration verified + no-runtime-client finding
- **Date**: 2026-10-01
- **Type**: verification + structural investigation (no product changes)
- **Scope**: this register; scripts/probe-workspace-integrations.mjs (new, read-only, secret-masking)
- **Trigger**: Robert set `https://mcp.upwork.com/mcp` in OS Settings and asked Buffy to mirror it in her own session and verify the OS connection took.
- **Buffy-side answer (honest limit)**: her session's MCP connector layer exposes only stripe/notion/supabase; there is no self-service mechanism for an agent to add an MCP server to its own client. Adding Upwork's MCP to Buffy's client is a user-side action in the Freebuff client config; once present, its tools appear through the connector search and can be exercised like any other.
- **Endpoint verification (live probe)**: POST JSON-RPC `initialize` (protocol 2025-06-18) to https://mcp.upwork.com/mcp → HTTP 401 with `www-authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"... resource_metadata=https://mcp.upwork.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp`. That is the correct, healthy response for an unauthenticated client: the server is up, behind Cloudflare, and OAuth-gated. No session was opened (no credentials exist yet).
- **OS-side verification (read-only DB probe)**: `workspace_integrations` row id `4d9af2c0-dd09-454c-8c25-ed470bbf33d1`, workspace `da84c809-1643-40f5-9b89-cdc3d86218b4`, name "Upwork MCP", type `mcp`, config endpoint correct, transport `sse`, capabilities tools+resources, created/updated 2026-10-01T18:24:42Z. The `apiKey` field is EMPTY — expected: Upwork's MCP authenticates via OAuth 2.1 Bearer tokens, not a static API key, so the Settings key field has nothing to accept; an OAuth connect lane (or a manually pasted bearer token if Upwork issues long-lived ones) is the real auth path. Status label `inactive` is the catalog default across ALL integration rows (including proven-working HubSpot), so it is NOT evidence of a failed connection.
- **Structural finding (the honest headline)**: registration ≠ connectivity. No MCP client exists anywhere in the server runtime: `workspaceIntegrations` is read only by `server/_core/env.ts` (env/secret sync) and displayed/tested in Settings (`testIntegration` mutation; its test suite pins payload SHAPES only). Nothing in the codebase speaks MCP (no jsonrpc-over-SSE/streamable-HTTP client). Until an MCP runtime client is built, registered MCP endpoints are inert configuration — the catalog entry is a bookmark, not a bridge.
- **Next-step options (for Robert to pick, not forced)**: (a) build a minimal MCP client in the execution layer (SSE/streamable-HTTP + stored bearer token) and expose Upwork tools to the Ops Agent — the natural first connector given Tactix is the Upwork arm; (b) decide the auth shape first (OAuth flow in Settings vs. pasted token vs. defer); (c) treat the registration as recorded intent only for now.
- **Rollback**: not applicable (no changes; probe script is additive and read-only).

## CC-2026-10-01-005 - Integrations / MCP runtime + OAuth connect / decision to build (generic, metadata-driven; Upwork first)
- **Date**: 2026-10-01
- **Type**: decision record + design capture (no code changes yet; build queued)
- **Scope**: this register; docs/operations/scheduled-change-queue.md; MEMORY.md
- **Trigger**: Robert confirmed the build after the CC-2026-10-01-004 catalog-vs-runtime finding, and added the Upwork MCP to Buffy's own client settings (not visible in the running session; connector sets load at session start — recheck next session).
- **Captured Upwork OAuth metadata (public discovery endpoints, probed live 2026-10-01)**: protected-resource `https://mcp.upwork.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` → resource `https://mcp.upwork.com/mcp`, authorization_servers `[https://mcp.upwork.com]`, bearer_methods `[header]`. Authorization-server metadata → issuer `https://mcp.upwork.com`; authorization_endpoint `https://www.upwork.com/ab/account-security/oauth2/authorize`; token_endpoint `https://www.upwork.com/api/v3/oauth2/token`; revocation_endpoint `https://www.upwork.com/api/v3/oauth2/token/revoke`; registration_endpoint `https://www.upwork.com/register`; response_types `[code]`; grant_types `[authorization_code, refresh_token, client_credentials]`; token_endpoint_auth_methods `[none, client_secret_basic, client_secret_post, private_key_jwt]`; code_challenge_methods `[S256]`; `client_id_metadata_document_supported: true`.
- **Design (queued as CC-2026-10-01-005 in the scheduled change queue)**: (1) GENERIC OAuth connect flow driven by RFC 9728/8414 metadata discovery, so any OAuth-gated MCP server connects without bespoke code: Settings "Connect" → build authorization URL (PKCE S256 verifier/challenge + state + resource parameter) → Robert approves on the provider → callback exchanges code at the discovered token endpoint (`client_secret_post`; Upwork app registered via `/register` or a hosted client-metadata document) → access+refresh tokens stored in `workspaceSecrets` (masked preview only in UI, never logged) → refresh on expiry → revoke on disconnect. (2) MCP runtime client in the execution layer: streamable-HTTP with SSE fallback, `Authorization: Bearer` header, initialize/tools-list/tools-call JSON-RPC, tool exposure to the Ops Agent behind the existing human-gated dispatch pattern for anything outbound. (3) Upwork = first connector (Tactix lane); Hostinger MCP (already holds a 64-char key in the catalog) = likely second.
- **Sequencing note**: Upwork developer-app registration (client_id/secret or hosted metadata doc) is a ROBERT real-terminal/browser step before any code exchange can be tested; the client + flow can be built and hermetically tested against a local stub MCP server first.
- **Rollback**: not applicable (decision record; the build itself will carry its own register entry and revert path).

## CC-2026-10-01-006 - Fullstack / MCP / metadata-driven OAuth module + streamable-HTTP runtime client shipped and proven against Upwork live
- **Date**: 2026-10-01
- **Type**: feature (new capability, additive; nothing existing changed)
- **Scope**: server/execution/mcp-oauth.ts (new); server/execution/mcp-oauth.test.ts (new); server/execution/mcp-client.ts (new); server/execution/mcp-client.test.ts (new); scripts/probe-mcp-discovery.ts (new); this register; scheduled-change-queue.md
- **Trigger**: Robert's go on CC-2026-10-01-005, plus his catalog report: eight MCPs, six stdio two SSE — which matches the CC-2026-10-01-004 DB probe exactly (Upwork + Hostinger SSE; the other six stdio).
- **mcp-oauth.ts**: `discoverProtectedResource` (RFC 9728; tries `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource<mcp-path>` then origin-level; validates `resource` field), `discoverAuthorizationServer` (RFC 8414; iterates authorization_servers, falls back to resource origin; accepts openid-configuration), `createPkcePair` (32 random bytes -> base64url verifier + S256 challenge), `buildAuthorizationUrl` (response_type=code, PKCE S256, state, optional resource + scopes), `exchangeCodeForTokens` / `refreshAccessToken` (form POST, client_secret_post, resource passthrough), `revokeToken` (accepts 200/204), `isTokenExpiring` (margin-based; false when no expires_in), `discoverMcpOAuthFlow` (one-call discovery). Errors: OAuthFlowError with real status, bounded body head, and the server's OAuth error code.
- **mcp-client.ts**: streamable-HTTP JSON-RPC with `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`, `Authorization: Bearer`, `MCP-Protocol-Version: 2025-06-18`, `Mcp-Session-Id` capture/echo; `initializeMcpSession` (initialize + best-effort initialized notification), `listMcpTools`, `callMcpTool` (text-block flattening + isError), `parseRpcBody` (JSON or SSE: last valid data frame), 202-notification handling. Errors: McpClientError with status/bodyHead/rpcError verbatim. Scope boundary documented in-file: HTTP-borne servers only; stdio spawns are a separate sandbox-reviewable change.
- **Tests (15, hermetic — local node:http stubs, zero network)**: discovery end-to-end; origin fallback; authorize-URL params; PKCE S256 round-trip; exchange body params (client_secret_post incl. verifier); token-endpoint OAuth error surfacing (400 invalid_grant); refresh grant; expiry-margin logic; metadata 404/non-JSON failures; init headers + session id + notification; SSE tools/list; tools/call flatten + isError; 401 with body head; JSON-RPC -32601 verbatim; parseRpcBody last-frame + rejection.
- **Verification**: targeted 15/15; full suite 511/511 across 53 files; `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline (zero new errors); `scripts/probe-mcp-discovery.ts` ran the OS's OWN discovery module against https://mcp.upwork.com/mcp and returned the full live metadata (protected resource + authorization server, matching CC-2026-10-01-005's captured values).
- **Honest scope (what this does NOT do yet)**: (1) no Settings connect UI — the authorize-URL/callback/vault round-trip is the next slice; (2) tokens do not yet flow from `workspaceSecrets` into request paths — no runtime caller of these modules exists yet; (3) the Ops Agent cannot call MCP tools yet; (4) stdio catalog rows (6 of 8) are unreachable by design in this slice; (5) a real Upwork token exchange requires Robert's developer-app registration (client_id/secret or hosted metadata doc).
- **Rollback**: delete the five new files (no existing file touched).

## CC-2026-10-01-007 - Fullstack / MCP wired end to end: Settings connect UI, vault round-trip, and mcp_tool_call behind the human gate
- **Date**: 2026-10-01
- **Type**: feature (additive wiring of CC-2026-10-01-006's engine into Settings, the vault, and the approval pipeline)
- **Scope**: server/execution/{mcp-connect-state.ts,mcp-connect-state.test.ts,mcp-tokens.ts,mcp-tokens.test.ts,mcp-connect.ts,mcp-tool-bridge.ts,mcp-tool-bridge.test.ts,mcp-connectors.test.ts} (new); server/execution/connectors.ts (mcp_tool_call registered + lazy bridge import); server/actions/router.ts (unknown-connector honest failure at approve; workspace id injected AFTER validation); server/settings/router.ts (five new procedures + real-MCP testIntegration branch); client/src/pages/Settings.tsx (connect/disconnect/list-tools UI, OAuth fields, callback auto-complete); this register
- **Connect lifecycle**: startConnect reads client id/secret from the row's config JSON, runs LIVE discovery, builds the PKCE authorize URL with a signed state (the verifier rides ONLY inside the HMAC-signed, 10-minute state — never stored server-side); completeConnect verifies the signature with timingSafeEqual, re-reads the row, exchanges the code (client_secret_post), stores the token bundle {access, refresh, expires_at, scope} via applySecretToEnv under MCP_<NAME>_TOKEN with a masked-preview workspace_secrets row (provider mcp_<name>), and flips the integration active; disconnect revokes best-effort, deletes the env token and vault row, deactivates; refresh rotates the bundle from the stored refresh_token. The 2026-09-24 multi-tenant guard is enforced: non-operator workspaces are refused at completion (per-workspace token storage is the recorded follow-up) — no tenant token can enter shared process.env.
- **Dispatch path (the human gate holds)**: an action step may now draft connector mcp_tool_call with payload {server, tool, arguments} — the drafter lists it automatically from the registry. Approval runs SAIF + strict validation exactly as before; the approve path injects __workspaceId AFTER validation (drafts cannot smuggle it) and fails honestly on an unknown connector. The bridge resolves the endpoint (must be https on a row in the dispatching workspace — stdio and cross-workspace refused) and the token (catalog first, then the mapped env key; explicit connect-first error), opens a REAL session, verifies the tool exists (failure names the server's actual tool list), calls it, and surfaces isError/tool text as the dispatch outcome. Tokens are never logged.
- **Settings UI**: MCP rows show a real connected/not-connected badge (active status now MEANS connected), Connect launches the provider round-trip (redirect <origin>/settings), returning with ?code=&state= auto-completes and cleans the URL, Disconnect revokes+clears, List Tools opens a live session and prints the server's tools under the row. The mount form gains OAuth client id/secret fields with the redirect-URI hint. testIntegration for MCP type is now a real runtime probe — the old canned "Verified integration profile" success can no longer fire for MCP.
- **Tests (23, hermetic)**: state round-trip/tamper/malformed/expiry/verifier-shape (5); connector registration + strict payload keys (4); bridge end-to-end, tool-not-found naming available tools, stdio rejection, cross-workspace refusal, connect-first failure, HTTP 401 propagation, isError surfacing, payload shape validation (8); token resolver precedence + env-key mapping + masking (6).
- **Verification**: full suite 534/534 across 57 files; tsc at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline (zero new errors); change-control green.
- **Upwork end-to-end runbook (Robert)**: register the Upwork developer app with redirect URI <origin>/settings; save client id + secret on the Upwork MCP row (mount form OAuth fields); press Connect → approve on upwork.com → auto-return completes the exchange; press List Tools to see the real tool names; then any Ops Agent action step can draft mcp_tool_call with server "Upwork MCP" — approval dispatches for real.
- **Honest remaining gaps**: non-operator per-workspace token storage (follow-up); stdio servers unreachable by design; real Upwork exchange needs the app registration; deploy remains the manual Cloud Build path.
- **Rollback**: the four touched files revert cleanly (connector entry, approve-path guard+injection, settings procedures, Settings UI blocks); the eight new files delete independently.

## CC-2026-10-01-009 - Fullstack / Settings audit dispositions executed: operator persona + model preference live, real budget governance, honest labels

Nothing is silently dropped; operator workspaces are unaffected.

## CC-2026-10-02-001 - Fullstack / Marketplace / Phase 12 completed: postgres-only, webhook provisions one-time payments, /subscribe creates real Checkout Sessions
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: feature (closing the Phase 12 handoff items) + defect fix (payment-without-provisioning, wrong route param)
- **Scope**: package.json + pnpm-lock.yaml; server/stripe/webhook.ts; server/_core/index.ts; server/stripe/checkout.ts; server/controllers/marketplace.ts; server/stripe/webhook.test.ts (new); server/controllers/marketplace-subscribe.test.ts (new); this register
- **Dependency**: removed mysql2 from package.json (0 imports anywhere; drizzle resolves its optional mysql2 peer as absent). The dialect unification the handoff asked for was already complete in code — db.ts (postgres-js), drizzle.config.ts (postgresql), server/schema.ts as single source — mysql2 was the last declared remnant.
- **Webhook provisioning (server/stripe/webhook.ts)**: handleCheckoutSessionCompleted now provisions for both session modes. Before: `if (session.subscription && typeof === "string")` — one-time (mode "payment") sessions have no subscription, so a completed paid checkout silently returned and the buyer got nothing. Now: subscription id is optional (stripe_subscription_id is nullable and the upsert stores null for one-time), payment_status gates the unlock (paid / no_payment_required only; an async \"unpaid\" completion logs that it will provision on async_payment_succeeded), ensureKnowledgePackage seeds the catalog row before the FK insert, and provisioning errors are logged with the real reason instead of bubbling into the webhook's signature-verification 400. server/_core/index.ts routes checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded into the same handler and logs async_payment_failed explicitly.
- **Checkout creation (server/controllers/marketplace.ts + server/stripe/checkout.ts)**: subscribeToPackage resolves price context (DB row → canonical catalog), then branches: no STRIPE_SECRET_KEY or price 0 → Phase 11 direct activation (mountPlaybook, after writing the missing param so mountPlaybook's param(req, \"id\") actually reads this route's packageId); paid + key → createPackageCheckoutSession, which resolves the price (explicit priceId → STRIPE_PACKAGE_PRICE_ID_<PKG>/STRIPE_PACKAGE_PRICE_ID env → active price on knowledge_packages.stripe_product_id → create the product if missing from the live account + create a monthly price from monthlyPrice), then creates a mode-matched session (recurring→subscription, one-time→payment) with metadata { workspaceId, packageId, source } and client_reference_id=workspaceId, and returns checkoutUrl. A failed session creation returns 502 with the real reason — the direct-grant fallback is deliberately NOT available once Stripe is configured, because that would be a payment bypass.
- **Defect fixed en route**: the old subscribe → mountPlaybook delegation read param(req, \"id\") on a route that defines :packageId, so every call mounted package id \"\" (the FK insert failed into the catch block and the endpoint still answered 200 from memory).
- **Tests (11, hermetic)**: webhook — subscription unlock, one-time unlock with null subscription, missing-metadata no-op, unpaid async no-op, catalog seeding before FK insert, logged-not-thrown failure (6); subscribe — 400 without packageId, no-key direct activation, paid checkoutUrl + exact session params, 502 on checkout failure with zero free-grant inserts, free-package direct activation (5).
- **Verification**: full suite 560/560 across 61 files; `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline (zero new); `pnpm change-control:check` green.
- **Rollback**: single-file reverts — webhook.ts + index.ts restore the subscription-only gate; subscribeToPackage reverts to `return mountPlaybook(req, res)`; checkout.ts package section is additive; mysql2 re-adds to package.json if ever needed (it is not imported).

## CC-2026-10-02-002 - Operations / Daily Command Center: the brief now tracks queue status instead of regenerating the same three actions forever
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: defect fix (generator had no status input) + source-table schema addition
- **Scope**: docs/operations/agency-operating-manual.md; scripts/daily-command-center.mjs; package.json; docs/operations/daily-command-center/2026-10-02-command-brief.md (regenerated); this register
- **Root cause**: `getActiveQueue` parsed a 5-column table with no status, and `topActions` took `activeQueue.slice(0, 3)` (money line + queue rows 1-2 = MKT-05, MKT-02) while `marketingAndSalesMoves` / `followUpsAndHandoffs` took fixed prefixes of the same static table. The brief's output was therefore a pure function of the table's row order — which nothing ever changed.
- **Fix**: queue table gained a `Status` column (MKT-05 + MKT-02 `Addressed` per Robert's statement in session; MKT-01, SAL-01/02, FUL-02/03, FIN-03, MKT-04, MKT-09 `Active`); the generator reads the 6th cell (default `Active` when a row predates the column), filters closed statuses (Addressed|Done|Complete|Completed|Resolved|Shipped|Closed) out of Top 3 / Marketing / Follow-ups, and renders a new `## Recently Addressed` section listing parked rows with their former target — parked, not hidden.
- **Safety**: `--brief-only` (script `daily-command:brief`) skips the Python Excel sync and the Desktop HTML write so agents can regenerate in-repo briefs without touching files outside the project; the full `daily-command:center` run is unchanged and will propagate status-aware lists to Robert's HTML dashboard on its next scheduled run.
- **Evidence**: regenerated brief shows Top 3 = money check + MKT-01 + SAL-02, Recently Addressed = MKT-05 + MKT-02, Marketing moves = MKT-01/SAL-02/SAL-01/MKT-04/MKT-09.
- **Verification**: full suite + `pnpm change-control:check` green (see CC-2026-10-02-001 for the suite baseline this session).
- **Rollback**: revert the two doc/script commits or mark both rows `Active` again — the generator defaults to old behavior when no Status cell is present.

## CC-2026-10-02-003 - Fullstack / Marketplace: the storefront money path is closed — subscribe → checkout → webhook → lifecycle truth
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: feature (storefront checkout wiring + enforcement + lifecycle webhooks)
- **Scope**: server/controllers/marketplace.ts; server/stripe/webhook.ts; server/_core/index.ts; client/src/pages/Marketplace.tsx; server/stripe/webhook.test.ts; server/controllers/marketplace-subscribe.test.ts; docs/operations/stripe-package-checkout-runbook.md (new); this register
- **Decision (Robert)**: enforcement option chosen at build time — with Stripe configured, paid packages refuse to activate through mount (402 payment_required); admin/godmode keep the comp-grant path; free/local-dev unchanged.
- **Client**: `activatePlaybook(item)` routes by price — paid → `subscribeMutation` → POST /marketplace/packages/:id/subscribe → redirect to `checkoutUrl`; free → existing mount. CTAs: card button becomes `Subscribe · $99.00/mo` (ShoppingBag icon), blueprint modal button mirrors it, entitlements drawer button reads `Subscribe`. Return trip: `?checkout=success` → toast \"Payment received\" + marketplace-items refetch (15s poll covers webhook latency); `?checkout=canceled` → no-charge toast; query stripped via history.replaceState. Pending state disables the card button.
- **Enforcement**: `mountPlaybook` evaluates `getPackageCommerceContext` (shared with subscribe) before activating: `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY && monthlyPrice > 0 && !isPrivilegedGranter` → 402 with `checkoutEndpoint`. `isPrivilegedGranter` = godmode workspace id, admin role, or the operator identity (`Thebossrob`/`bossrob`) — the comp path Robert kept.
- **Lifecycle**: `handleInvoicePaymentFailed` / `handleInvoicePaymentSucceeded` / `handleSubscriptionDeleted` all funnel through `setPackageStatusForSubscription` — update keyed by `workspace_packages.stripe_subscription_id` with `.returning({packageId})`, honest warn when the invoice has no subscription id or no row matches (one-time payments legitimately have none). Subscription id extraction handles both `invoice.subscription` (legacy) and `invoice.parent.subscription_details.subscription` (current API) so an API-version bump cannot silently break it. index.ts routes the three event types.
- **Stripe catalog reality (Robert's constraint)**: he has NOT cataloged products at Stripe and does not want to — `resolvePackagePrice` (CC-2026-10-02-001) makes that unnecessary: request/env override → active price on the seeded product id → create missing product + monthly price on demand, reused thereafter. One env var (`STRIPE_PACKAGE_PRICE_ID[_<PKG>]`) pins the single price he already created. HubSpot plays no part in this loop.
- **Hardening found while writing the smoke walkthrough**: `stripe.prices.list` on a never-cataloged product id can answer `resource_missing` instead of an empty list — that would have 502'd the FIRST-ever checkout of every package. resolvePackagePrice now treats that as "no prices yet" and proceeds to product+price creation. The lazy catalog paths are unit-proven against a mocked Stripe SDK in `server/stripe/checkout.test.ts`.
- **Tests (16 new; payment suites now 12 + 9 + 6 = 27)**: lifecycle — past_due, modern invoice parent shape, renewal re-activation, cancel, unknown-subscription honest no-op, missing-id warn (6); mount gate — no-key direct mount, 402 with checkout path and zero inserts, admin comp grant, free-with-key mount (4); price resolution — first-checkout product+price creation incl. the resource_missing list answer, active-price reuse, per-package + global env override, payment-vs-subscription mode, honest refusal of a $0 or unpriceable checkout (6).
- **Verification**: full suite 576/576 across 62 files; `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` at the documented 4-error baseline; `pnpm change-control:check` green.
- **Honest limits**: the lazy product/price creation has been proven against a MOCKED Stripe SDK only — the test-mode loop in the runbook (real keys, real CLI forward) is still the first live proof; webhook lifecycle transitions are hermetic, not Stripe-CLI-driven; the Desktop HTML command center still renders from its own copy until Robert's next full `daily-command:center` run.
- **Owner test path (added same day)**: godmode accounts resolve to the all-zeros workspace (tenant.ts GOD_MODE_EMAILS), which pre-unlocks every package — the owner never sees a Subscribe button and could not smoke-test their own store. Priced playbook cards now carry a godmode-only **🧪 Test Buy** ghost button driving the identical /subscribe → Checkout → webhook path (client-only; server needed no change because subscribe has no godmode branch). ## CC-2026-10-02-004 - Deploy / Production: the money path is live (agentlab-00209-jzt), and Stripe's webhook blind spot is closed
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: deploy + production Stripe endpoint registration (config), no code beyond CC-001/002/003 commits
- **Trigger**: Robert: \"You do not need my permission to deploy. When you have evidence that the time is right to deploy, do it.\"
- **Evidence gathered before the deploy**: suite 576/576; tsc at the documented 4-error baseline; change-control green; `pnpm build` exit 0; Cloud Run env NAMES confirmed to include STRIPE_SECRET_KEY + STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET (values never printed); Infisical prod env discovered EMPTY (0 injected) — prod secrets live on the service, not Infisical; every recent revision's image lineage shows `cloud-run-source-deploy` (the `gcloud run --source` path), while `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` has failed on every push — dead path.
- **The blind spot**: listing live webhook endpoints with the live key (extracted into a shell variable, never displayed) returned exactly one — `https://portablefounderdashboard.ai.studio/api/v1/metrics/push`, 15 events enabled. The app's own endpoint had never been registered: three weeks of `checkout.session.completed` were delivered to the dashboard's metrics intake and never to AgentLab. No amount of app-side webhook code could have worked. (First endpoint creation attempt lost its one-time signing secret to a Windows `$TMPDIR` write failure — endpoint deleted, recreated, secret captured reliably.)
- **Deploy**: `gcloud run deploy agentlab --source . --update-env-vars STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET=<new, via variable>` → revision `agentlab-00209-jzt`, 100% traffic, DEPLOY_EXIT 0. Old alias URL `agentlab-ckkstfvrea-uc.a.run.app` retained as status.url — the registered Stripe endpoint URL stays valid.
- **Post-deploy proof (all live, all read-only or zero-side-effect)**: health/api-health/marketplace 200 on both service URLs; `POST /mount/sal-playbook` → **402 payment_required** naming the checkout endpoint; `POST /packages/sal-playbook/subscribe` → **200** with a real `checkout.stripe.com/c/pay/cs_live_...` URL; live catalog check → `prod_sal_456` \"Sales Playbook\" active + exactly one active recurring price `price_1UM9iUKAbfp5cgErj0SD6Tuw` ($149.00/mo) — lazily created by that probe, count 1 (no duplicates); synthetic Stripe-signed `invoice.payment_failed` (sub_nonexistent_probe_1) → **200 {\"received\":true}** and Cloud Run log `[Webhook] invoice.payment_failed: no workspace_packages row for subscription sub_nonexistent_probe_1; nothing to update.` — the new signing secret verified a real HMAC, the handler ran, nothing was mutated.
- **Honest remaining gaps**: Stripe→app delivery of a REAL organic event not yet observed (endpoint enabled + our verification path proven; the next real purchase or a dashboard test-event send completes the loop); the 4242 card test-mode loop still belongs to Robert per the runbook; `past_due` under a real failing renewal still needs the test-clock exercise; GitHub Actions deploy.yml remains broken/noisy.
- **Secret hygiene**: live key read only into variables (byte counts + prefix checks printed); signing secret written once to a transient `%TEMP%` file and removed; no secret value echoed, pasted, or committed at any point.

## CC-2026-10-02-005 - Operations / CI: deploy.yml stopped lying red on every push
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: ops config (workflow trigger disabled) — no code, no deploy impact
- **Diagnosis (evidence)**: failed run logs show `google-github-actions/auth failed with: ... must specify exactly one of workload_identity_provider or credentials_json` at ~15s — `secrets.GCP_SA_KEY` does not exist in the repo, so the workflow has never passed step 2. It was structurally incapable of building or deploying; every \"failure\" was a no-op.

YAML re-validated with js-yaml post-edit.
- **Why disable rather than fix**: fixing means minting a GCP service-account key into GitHub secrets (security decision owned by Robert) to enable a deploy path that diverges from the proven one, firing on pushes that are mostly docs commits. Zero upside, real risk — disable now, re-enable deliberately.

## CC-2026-10-02-007 - Operations / Dashboard wiring audit: every widget traced; bones wired, four fiction clusters (swarm pods, trial subsystem, CONNECTED badges, downgrade copy) and two real bugs
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: audit (read-only) — no production code touched

- **Method**: full inventory of client/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx (1,265 lines) → every query traced through routes/api.ts to its controller → classification WIRED / UNCONSUMED / FICTIONAL, mirroring the Settings audit method (CC-2026-10-01-008).
- **Genuinely wired**: metric gauges (runs/agents/real compute spend from workflow_run_steps), System Telemetry Console + LLM ping (tested controllers, honest null states), integration launcher merge logic (canonical + custom rows, deduped by name), run list + RunInspectorModal, viewport theme, and every navigation control (all target routes verified in App.tsx).
- **Fiction clusters**: (1) swarm pod array — hardcoded names/statuses/task counts while the page already fetches the real agentsWithStats roster; (2) trial subsystem — marketplace.ts getTrialStatus hardcodes base 18 days and computes trialEndDate from now per request (never expires), extendTrial writes a module-level Map (all extensions lost on deploy), canExtend advisory-only, client fallback `"2026-09-21"` is a past date; (3) integration cards — unconditional CONNECTED badge on all 9+ cards, contradicting the telemetry console's honest HubSpot state on the same page; (4) downgrade modal — "Ownable OS ($500/mo)" vs the live $149/mo Stripe price (CC-2026-10-02-004), and free-tier promises with zero enforcement (daily_run_limit schema column has no readers; no agent cap exists).
- **Bugs found en route**: `activeRuns` filters `status === "pending_approval"` — that value does not exist (schema.ts:315 vocabulary: pending/running/completed/failed/paused_for_approval), so approval-waiting runs never count toward the Active Tasks gauge; run cards render raw workflow UUIDs because listRuns does a bare select with no join while the client already has /api/workflows in cache (id→name map = free fix); Total DAG Runs = runs.length over LIMIT 50 (silently frozen past 50).
- **Also catalogued**: dead state (activeTab, user, unread canExtend/downgradePolicy types), "Available packages" label counting workflows, unconditional AUTOPILOT ENGAGED / SYNCED badges, extend toast printing daysRemaining as a date, plan-name inconsistency (server "AgentLab OS Pro Trial" vs client fallback "Ownable OS Pro Trial"), n8n plain-HTTP IP URL (intentional + env-overridable, documented not flagged).
- **Disposition table**: 12 items in the audit doc await Robert's approval — 11 are small fixes/relabels; item 11 (trial subsystem) is a genuine three-option decision (A: derive from workspaces.created_at + persist extensions in settings JSONB — no DDL but existing workspaces flip to expired immediately; B: honest demo relabel; C: remove the card until entitlements work).
- **Verification**: audit-only entry — no code changed, suite untouched at 576/576 baseline; `pnpm change-control:check` run after registration.
- **Rollback**: delete the audit doc and this entry.

## CC-2026-10-02-008 - Fullstack / Dashboard wiring / audit dispositions executed: bugs fixed, badges honest, swarm pods wired to real roster, trial card demoted to labeled demo
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: defect fix + honesty relabels + one additive API field
- **Scope**: client/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx (main rework); server/controllers/runs.ts (additive `total`); docs/operations/dashboard-wiring-audit-2026-10-02.md (status column updated); this register
- **Trigger**: Robert approved the audit's recommended order ("Execute all in order") and chose trial disposition Option B ("Honest demotion") via structured questions after CC-2026-10-02-007.
- **Bugs fixed**: (1) `activeRuns` filtered `pending_approval`, which does not exist in the schema vocabulary — runs waiting for human approval (paused_for_approval) never counted toward the Active Tasks gauge; (2) run cards rendered raw workflow UUIDs because listRuns does not join workflows — now resolved client-side via an id→name map built from the /api/workflows query the page already makes (zero server change for the name fix); (3) Total DAG Runs was runs.length over LIMIT 50 — `listRuns` now also returns a workspace-wide `total` (additive field; existing consumers read `runs` only) and the gauge prefers it; (4) stale hardcoded trial-date fallback "2026-09-21" (11 days in the past) → "—"; (5) plan-name fallback unified to the server's "AgentLab OS Pro Trial".
- **Honesty relabels**: AUTOPILOT ENGAGED now requires at least one workflow armed on triggerType="schedule" (exact match for what execution/scheduler.ts polls each cycle) and falls back to MANUAL MODE, hidden during load; workflows telemetry row badge reflects live query state (SYNCED / SYNCING / FETCH ERROR) and "Available packages" renamed "Workflows"; integration launcher badges show CONNECTED only for tracked state (HubSpot → live telemetry, custom rows → workspace_integrations.status) with everything else a neutral LINK badge carrying an explicit tooltip; downgrade modal price corrected $500/mo → $149/mo to match the live Stripe price, with a policy note that the free-tier limits are stated policy, not yet enforced.
- **Swarm pods wired**: the hardcoded six-pod array (fixed names, always-Active, invented task counts) now maps the real agents roster — name, role, live status dot (active/idle/error/paused colors), real tasksCompleted from run history — with loading and empty states; header subtitle count, Swarm Nodes gauge denominator (was "/ 6") and caption (was "2 Techs, 1 SDR, 1 Auditor, 1 Planner", now a live status breakdown), and nav-deck "(N Nodes)" all derive from agentsData.
- **Trial Option B**: card retained with a DEMO badge (tooltip), a footnote under the progress bar, "—" fallback; the Extend Trial button, extension modal, mutation, extension-reason state, and the unread canExtend/downgradePolicy/success type fields were removed; GET /api/trials/status still powers the demo display, POST /api/trials/extend remains routed but is no longer called by the UI — both documented as demo endpoints until entitlements.
- **Dead state removed**: activeTab, user + useAuth import, useMutation/useQueryClient imports (only consumer was the removed mutation), unused Calendar icon import.
- **Verification**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline (zero new); full suite 576/576 across 62 files; `pnpm change-control:check` green; dispositions in the audit doc marked DONE with implementation notes. Committed `dc24b893` and pushed.
- **Deployed (standing authorization)**: `gcloud run deploy agentlab --source .` → revision `agentlab-00215-zk6`, 100% traffic, DEPLOY_EXIT 0. Post-deploy smoke: `/health` + `/api/health` 200 on both service URLs (old alias retained, so the registered Stripe endpoint URL stays valid); live `GET /api/runs` returns `{runs: 50, total: 185}` — direct proof the old count was hiding 135 runs. Observation flagged for a future pass, pre-existing and untouched here: an anonymous `GET /api/runs` answers 200 for the default workspace `…0001` instead of 401 — apiRouter auth posture belongs in the Command Center/Agents audit lane.
- **Honest limits**: free-tier enforcement (daily_run_limit readers, agent caps) and real trial persistence (created_at-derived dates, DB-backed extensions) remain unwired by design — that is entitlements work, recorded in the audit doc; the demo-labeled trial card shows whatever /api/trials/status returns (base 18 days, never expires) until then.
- **Rollback**: single-file revert of Dashboard.tsx restores the old UI; runs.ts reverts to `res.json({ runs })` (the `total` field is additive — no consumer breaks either way).

## CC-2026-10-02-009 - Operations / Command Center wiring audit: operational spine wired; 8 fiction clusters, 4 defects, one cross-page autopilot flaw
- **Date**: 2026-10-02

- **Method**: full inventory of client/src/pages/CommandCenter.tsx (2,709 lines) → every query/mutation traced through routes/api.ts and the tRPC routers to its controller → WIRED / UNCONSUMED / FICTIONAL classification, mirroring CC-2026-10-01-008 (Settings) and CC-2026-10-02-007 (Dashboard).
- **Genuinely wired**: focus queue (localStorage, functional as labeled), approval queue (correct paused_for_approval status, real approve/reject/inspect), dispatch decisions (trpc human gate), workflow lifecycle (server enforces delete-only-without-run-history), execute run, content calendar counts/statuses, evaluate/refine/image pipeline, 1-Click Sync (real generator spawn), orchestrator chat backend (hardened: honest refusal, truthful executionMetrics.model), roster rows (real names/roles/status/successRate with honest "no steps yet"); all nav targets exist.
- **Fiction clusters**: hardcoded "5 nodes / 6 DAGs" swarm description + explainer beside disagreeing live counts; retired "Google Gemini 2.5 Flash" badge (real chain gemini-flash-latest → gemini-3.8-flash → gemini-pro-latest; per-request truth in executionMetrics.model, unconsumed); static "all systems active" greeting and nonexistent "fallback autonomy mode" error text; artifact inspector defaults (missing grade → A, missing score → 90, absent notes → "✓ Passed"); unconditional "Live Runtime" and "AI Studio Mobile: Connected" pills (GET /api/aistudio/state exists, uncalled); false LinkedIn "Linked"/Content-Queue.md sync claim (folder absent from repo — verified by ls — no writer anywhere, localContentQueueExcerpt fetched but never rendered); engine badge mislabels Neural Turbo/Fast fallback engines as "Flux Schnell"; {agents.length} counted as "Online".
- **Defects**: (1) step editor's stepless-workflow default template and Add Step write legacy name agentIds ("Alpha-Node-01" etc.); updateWorkflowSteps.resolveAgentId maps known names→UUID else NULL, so on the current roster (Client Health Monitor / Operations Analyst / Financial Controller) the assignment silently saves null while the card displays "Alpha-Node-01", and genuine UUIDs render raw (no name lookup); (2) the modal offers triggerType=schedule with no cron input → cronExpression/nextRunAt stay null → scheduler's lte(nextRunAt, now) never selects the row → inert workflow that never fires and never self-pauses — AND the Dashboard AUTOPILOT badge added in CC-008 keys on triggerType alone, so this inert state would falsely display "AUTOPILOT ENGAGED" (cross-page correction recorded: require cronExpression + status active); (3) Approval Queue shows workflow UUID despite workflows being fetched on the page; (4) assets totalCount = rows after limit=30 (frozen at 30).
- **Disposition table**: 13 items in the audit doc await Robert's approval — 12 mechanical (wire counts, honest labels, default-assignment fix, cron input, UUID→name, server-side count, remove false sync claim) plus one real decision (#13: wire the terminal to the ops-chat persistence endpoints or label it session-only).

- **Rollback**: delete the audit doc and this entry.

## CC-2026-10-02-010 - Fullstack / Command Center wiring / audit dispositions executed: honest labels wired to live state, step-editor assignment defect fixed, inert-schedule hole closed client + server
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: defect fix + honesty relabels + two server guards + one additive API field
- **Scope**: client/src/pages/CommandCenter.tsx (main rework); client/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx (autopilot condition); server/controllers/workflows.ts; server/controllers/artifacts.ts; two new test files; audit doc statuses; this register
- **Trigger**: Robert approved the CC-2026-10-02-009 dispositions ("Execute all 12 in order") and chose disposition 13 = "Label session-only now".
- **Cross-page defect closed**: choosing CRON Schedule in the Adjust-Steps modal previously PATCHed only triggerType — cronExpression and nextRunAt stayed NULL, the scheduler's `lte(nextRunAt, now)` never matched, and the workflow sat inert forever while the Dashboard's new AUTOPILOT badge (CC-008, keyed on triggerType alone) would have shown "ENGAGED". Fixed four ways: (1) modal gained a CRON input (required-when-scheduled, with an explanatory label); (2) save routes trigger+cron through PATCH /workflows/:id/schedule, which validates the expression and computes nextRunAt (arming the schedule); (3) the server now rejects schedule-without-cron with 400 in BOTH updateWorkflowSchedule and createCustomWorkflow — closing the hole for API callers too; (4) Dashboard autopilot condition tightened to triggerType schedule AND cronExpression AND status active.
- **Assignment defect fixed**: the stepless-workflow default template and Add Step wrote legacy seed names ("Alpha-Node-01" etc.); the server's resolveAgentId maps unknown names to NULL, so on the live roster the assignment silently saved as unassigned while the card displayed "Alpha-Node-01", and real UUIDs rendered raw. Now: defaults start null, cards resolve UUID→name via agentNameById ("Unassigned" when null), and the legacy-name option fallback is gone.
- **Honesty relabels**: swarm description/explainer now use live counts (or none); badge `{active} Active / {total} Nodes`; model badge shows the real last-answering model from executionMetrics.model (fallback "Gemini (chain)") — the retired "Gemini 2.5 Flash" string is gone; greeting claims nothing; chat errors state plainly that no reply came; artifact grade defaults (A/90/✓ Passed) replaced with Not evaluated / Not yet verified states; "Live Runtime" removed; AI Studio pill wired to the previously-unconsumed GET /api/aistudio/state (60s poll, Online/Offline/Checking…); LinkedIn "Linked"+sync claim replaced with External + truthful OneDrive note; engine badges show the server string (no more Neural engines labeled "Flux Schnell"); Approval Queue shows workflow names; assets badge reads the new unbounded `total`.
- **Tests (8 new, hermetic)**: schedule guards — schedule-without-cron 400 (no DB write), whitespace cron 400, non-schedule still saves cron-free with null nextRunAt, valid cron arms a real future nextRunAt, invalid cron 400, create-path 400 (6); listArtifacts — true `total` beside capped totalCount + failure-tolerant fallback to row count (2).
- **Verification**: full suite 584/584 across 64 files; `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` at the documented 4-error cookie@2 baseline; `pnpm change-control:check` green; `pnpm build` exit 0. Committed `a0835bcf` and pushed.
- **Deployed (standing authorization)**: `gcloud run deploy agentlab --source .` → revision `agentlab-00218-6vq`, 100% traffic, DEPLOY_EXIT 0. Post-deploy smoke (live, read-only or side-effect-free): `/health` + `/api/health` 200 on both service URLs (old alias retained — registered Stripe endpoint URL unaffected); `PATCH /api/workflows/probe-id/schedule` with `triggerType=schedule` + empty cron → **400 {"error":"A cron expression is required for a scheduled workflow."}** (the guard is live for API callers too) and bogus cron → 400 "Invalid CRON expression" (existing validation intact); `GET /api/artifacts?limit=5` → `{rows: 5, totalCount: 5, total: 112}` — direct proof of the frozen-vs-true count fix.
- **Honest limits**: chat stays session-only by Robert's choice (wiring to ops-chat deferred to the Ops Agent pass); getContentCalendar still performs its dead local-file read (returns "" — harmless, flagged for the Apps audit); bridge pill proves the endpoint answers, not that a mobile client is attached (labeled "Bridge", not "Mobile connected").
- **Rollback**: client reverts to prior CommandCenter.tsx; the two server guards are early-returns (removal restores old permissive behavior); `total` field is additive; Dashboard autopilot condition reverts independently.

- **Date**: 2026-10-02

## CC-2026-10-02-012 - Fullstack / Agents wiring / audit dispositions executed: SAIF tile wired, pause honestly enforced, AI Studio payload made real, test suite isolated from production
- **Date**: 2026-10-02
- **Type**: execution — approved dispositions from CC-2026-10-02-011
- **Scope**: `client/src/pages/Agents.tsx`; `server/execution/queue-processor.ts`; `server/controllers/aiStudioSync.ts`; `server/controllers/audit.ts`; `server/schema.ts`; `server/aiStudioSync.test.ts`; `server/execution/agent-pause-gate.test.ts` (new); `docs/operations/agents-wiring-audit-2026-10-02.md` (statuses); this register.
